# Vampire Survivors Wiki (vampire.survivors.wiki) — Enemigos y jefes
Extraido 2026-10-07. 401 paginas. Se quitaron Gallery, Update history y matrices de iconos. "| a | b |" = fila de tabla; [Nombre] = icono; "## " = encabezado. Los otros temas estan en otros archivos.

| {"stages":"Mazerella","update":"Vampire Survivors: Ante Chamber out now, for free!","dlc":"","resistances":"Freeze, Rosary, Debuff","image":"","health":"2000","movespeed":"117","release":"28 October 2025","skills":"HP x Level","name":"17th Collossus","damage":"40"}
← AccumulaTori17th CollossusBat Dragon →
“
What happened to the previous sixteen Colossi? They're an urban legend, officially considered fiction despite persistent rumors. Why the heck did we start at number seventeen for this one, then? I'm seriously asking.
We know one thing for sure - the Colossus is a beautiful and peaceful beast, beloved by all. Can you imagine ever hurting something so precious, so innocent, so dedicated to the art of minding its own adorable business? You'd have to be a world class coglione to even consider it.
Almost as bad as letting a horse fall to its presumed death without bothering to see if it's okay.
— Ars Gouda, p. 168
17th Collossus is an enemy that appears in Mazerella.

## Trivia
- The 17th Collossus' name and bestiary description are a reference to the 17th Colossus, an urban legend surrounding the game Shadow of the Colossus.

| {"type":"Normal","resistances":"Freeze, Rosary, Debuff","update":"Vampire Survivors: Ode to Castlevania is out now!","dlc":"oc","notes":"","image":"Sprite-Abbadon.png","stages":"Ode to Castlevania","movespeed":"140","release":"31 October 2024","skills":"HP x Level","health":"240","damage":"10"}
← TreantAbbadonMenace →
“
Summoning hordes of lucusts to attack its enemies in the clergy, Abbadon has long been reviled as a sects pest. He treats plagues as performances, directing swarmed with his and as a conductor would an orchestra. Very stylish for a guy who doesn't wear trousers.
— Ars Gouda, p. Oc 43
Abbadon is an enemy that appears in Ode to Castlevania, within the final north part of the castle, and spawns when the plate with the skull symbol is pressed. 
Upon defeating Abbadon for the first time, he will drop the  King's Gate relic.
Defeating Abbadon while playing as  Soma Cruz unlocks  Yoko Belnades.

← Pol.lo RossoAbraxasAbraxas Phronesis →
“
Is it a god, is it a demon, or is it just a chicken that got really, really big? Science has suggested the latter, but contractual obligation to make this book as spicy as possible suggests some fowl combination of the first two things, thus satisfying the further obligation to refer to at least one creature from the Coop as "fowl."
Who doesn't love a bit of birdplay?
— Ars Gouda, p. 78
Abraxas is an enemy that appears in The Coop. It first appears as a boss in that stage before appearing as a normal enemy.

Chicken Wizardry is one of the oldest forms of magic a sage can perform. Some say it's just regular magic with a tenuously shoehorned bird theme, but take not lightly the dark powers of peckromancy. The finest of the feathered fiends, moving gracefully even while stamping on their victium' skulls with such brutality we coined the term "head porridge." Many heads have been porridged, and at least one was mueslied, in the merciless march of the Abraxas Crown.

A fully morphed Torino, artificially evolved by lactomantic magicks. While they no longer yield dairy produce, any milk they consume is processed iun the digestive system and comes out as a delicious spreadable.

← Evil SnowmanAlien CentipedeGulcan Tank →
“
Birthed through Colonel Bahamut's biotechnological experiments with the Alien Cell, these multilegged monstrosities just love crawling around. Though they may seem aggressive, Alien Centipedes are simply obsessed with scurrying, unaware of the effects their spiky, toxic bodies have on impacted bystanders. Were they not currently being deployed as weapons of traitorous warfare these beasts would make challenging but rewarding pets, or at least an excellent source of protein.
— Ars Gouda, p. Og 5

← AlrauneAnti-AntMoch →
“
Their name isn't mere wordplay (heavens forbid we start doing puns) but an allusion to the very real hatred they feel towards ants. Anti-Ants hate the "mushroomy" Naml of Cordycep, coined the cruel moniker "Lesser Ants" for those in Wichdom Pulchra, and even found a way to travel through the Junction to insult the esoteric Space Ants.
They claim they're not ant racists, they just want Anti-Ant Land for the Anti-Ants. They're total ant racists, and frankly their views on caterpillars are pretty disgusting too.
— Ars Gouda, p. Ed 16
Anti-Ant is an enemy that appears in Emerald Diorama. It appears as a normal enemy and a boss.

← Demon BeastArchdemonTrinacria →
“
Big. Red. Horny. It really doesn’t get much more demonic than that. Arch Demons strive to exemplify all that it means to be a demon, and they’re diabolically good at it. They swear a lot, too.

Archdemon is an enemy that appears in Cappella Magna and Boss Rash. In Cappella Magna, it appears as both a normal enemy and a boss. In Boss Rash, it only appears as a boss.
Archdemon takes reduced knockback. Its boss variant's health is also base on the player's level.

| {"stages":"Red Moon Manor","update":"Legacy of the Bloodmoon is out now!","dlc":"bm","resistances":"Freeze, Rosary, Debuff, Knockback","image":"","health":"1,500 - 75,000","movespeed":"160","release":"28 August 2026","skills":"-","name":"Ashtart","damage":"25"}
← CongregationAshtartSargon →
“
Ashtart was already deep in her grey years when she became one of the followers of Baal'Thasar. In fact, she was among the first.
She lived in fear that, on the day she died, she would have to face the judgement of some merciless god for the unspeakable sins she had committed in life.
Her desperation to avoid the inevitable led her to become the first to experiment with human sacrifices, twisted rituals, and all sorts of abhorrent practices, all at the expense of the lesser disciples of the Order of the Red Moon. She would consume their organs, wear their skin, graft their organs into herself. With every abominable act, she lost another piece of herself, and plunged her soul deeper into the depths of damnation.
As her grotesque figure wandered apathically through the halls of Red Moon Manor, her dead cold eyes forever searching for the next victim of some vile experiment, a group of terrified disciples of the Order decided to put her at the center of one instead.
It did not move. It did not even scream. The soulless abomination that Ashtart had become simply kept throwing its dagger-like stare around, searching for the next piece of flesh to consume, while its body burned in bright flames and sharp swords plunged again and again into its abhorrent mass of organs.
The relief of the disciples did not last long. The charred bones of Ashtart rose from a pile of cinders, and, somehow, even without her eyes, the disciples could feel her feral gaze picking them, one by one.
No matter how deeply her remains are buried, how thoroughly her bones are shattered, or how many exorcisms are cast upon her, Ashtart's corrupted will always comes back, clutching at anything it can control.
An unstoppable presence. Dark and hollow. Mindless and soulless. An evil presence with the singular goal of preying on life.
— Ars Gouda, p. Bm 18
Ashtart is an enemy that appears in Red Moon Manor. She appears when the players enters the manor, and her location will be shown as a question mark (?) on the map, which will be removed after her appearance.

Avatar Infernas is a special enemy that appears in inverse Inlaid Library. Defeating Avatar Infernas for the first time unlocks him as a playable character.
Unlike most enemies, Avatar does not spawn as a part a wave or map event, but spawns under certain conditions in inverted Inlaid Library after obtaining Yellow Sign and Greatest Jubilee.
Avatar Infernas is resistant to knockback, freeze, instant kill effects, such as Pentagram and Rosary, and debuffs.

| {"resistances":"Freeze, Debuff, Knockback","image":"Sprite-Avatar of Gaea.png","stages":"Lake Foscari","movespeed":"140","release":"13 April 2023","health":"8","update":"New DLC & Patch 1.4.0","damage":"5"}
← Ghostly ApparitionAvatar of GaeaCauld →
“
When the very natural order is under threat, a great coalescence of all life’s benevolence manifests in corporeality a being of such grace and beauty as to bring civilizations to their knees in awe. The purest embodiment of that which is virtuous and true, the very heart of the planet given form. It once caved a man’s head in with its hoof for peeing in the lake. Blood and bits of skull everywhere.
— Ars Gouda, p. Fs 21
Avatar of Gaea is a boss that appears in Lake Foscari. It gains more health based on the amount of enemies killed this run. Defeating it in Lake Foscari unlocks the stage's hyper mode. It takes reduced knockback and it is resistant to freeze and debuffs.
It also makes an appearance in the special starting event of The Coop, assuming the player did not kill any  Chickenfantries for an entire minute for the first time. In that stage, they are not an enemy or boss that can be killed.
## Behavior
It starts off with very little health and once it is defeated, it becomes invulnerable. After a while, it starts "healing", then based on [(enemies killed / 1,000 x 0.2 seconds), at most 10 seconds + 2 more], multiple events happens:
- The enemy minimum is increased from 1 to 150, meaning more can spawn at once.
- 5 seconds after the initial countdown, Avatar of Gaea starts to spawn its own Ghostly Apparitions every 0.1 seconds 101 times (for 10 seconds).
- It finally stops being invulnerable.
It increases its max health by the number of enemies killed in this run. Its health and damage are also increased by the current character's golden egg bonuses if they are selected, it gains 1 max health per Max Health bonus and 0.01 power per 1% Might bonus. The max health increase is instantaneous and the healing effect is purely visual. Each time it "heals" by 1,000, representing 1,000 kills, which happens every 0.1 seconds; it also grows bigger by 1% additively for each heal.

# Baal'Thasar (enemy)
This article is about enemy. For character, see Baal'Thasar.
DLC Content (Legacy of the Bloodmoon)
This article primarily features content only available in DLC, which may not be available in all copies of the game.
| Baal'Thasar [DLC]
| 
| Bestiary ?Enemy stats as displayed in the Bestiary.See individual variants for more detailed information.

| HP | 80,000-100,000

| Resistances | Freeze, Rosary, Debuff, Knockback
| Skills | -
| Found in | Red Moon Manor
| {"stages":"Red Moon Manor","update":"Legacy of the Bloodmoon is out now!","dlc":"bm","resistances":"Freeze, Rosary, Debuff, Knockback","image":"","health":"80,000-100,000","movespeed":"150","release":"28 August 2026","skills":"-","name":"Baal'Thasar","damage":"20"}
← SargonBaal'ThasarChompo →
“
A rare philosopher with a gentle belief in the goodness of life, Baal'Thasar was also a healer and preacher who wanted to help and uplift the people around him.
And then came the first Blood Moon.
When asked what he saw during the event, Baal'Thasar's words would turn to riddles and fanatical ramblings, strange for someone once so eloquent. People said the event had driven him mad, as he began to subtly steer his teachings toward talks of eternal life and infinite irches, culminating in the founding of the Order of the Red Moon.
The truth is that the first Blood Moon blessed Baal'Thasar with a perilous gift. New eyes. Eyes that allowed him to see the duplicity, the avarice, and the selfish hunger of the people who came to him for comfort and cure. Eyes that allowed him to see life from the shallow, self-centered point of view of the desperate souls around him.
As he found himself surrounded by those dark, coiling threads of cupidity, Baal'Thasar ultimately lost sight of his own guiding light. He grased the venomous tethers with both hands, let them bind him, let all of his being become a channel for the insatiable want that he saw in the world.
Maybe he started with good intentions. Maybe he thought he could unravel the serpentine threads, eradicate them, shine his own light upon them.
But his light was quickly eclipsed, and the only thing Baal'Thasar discovered was that there is no origin and no terminus to greed. It is an insatiable, seductive beast that eats everything and serves only itself.
— Ars Gouda, p. Bm 20
Baal'Thasar is an enemy that appears in Red Moon Manor.
It is located in the west of the manor, where  Congregation spawns, on the center of the labyrinth, up from the  Velvet Dodecahedron. The player will find a pentagram that will teleport them to a room similar to the stage Room 1665. Where the player will fight trough hordes of enemies before fighting Baal'Thasar himself.

| {"stages":"Mad Forest, Il Molise","update":"Vampire Survivors: Legacy of the Bloodmoon is coming this summer!","name":"Bat Dragon","resistances":"Freeze, Rosary, Debuff, Knockback","image":"","health":"1000","movespeed":"110","release":"6 June 2026","skills":"HP x Level","theme":"It Stares Back","damage":"20"}
← 17th CollossusBat DragonBat Drakelet →
“
Sinister shadows fall over the generic Italian countryside, flapping, flaming, and freakishly bloody big. Some say that the first vampire was the child of a dragon, but as nobody thus far has ever conducted an interview or an ancestry test with a vampire, I'm inclined to believe "Bat Dragon" is just a title for a very large bat, and dragons aren't real.
What's certain is that anyone bold or foolish enough to disturb one of these monstrosities is surely marked for death, as the Bat Dragon uses its size, speed, and sonar to relentlessly chase its targets until its thirst for their blood is slaked. The Bat Dragon's hematophagy and use of sonar almost certainly confirm that they aren't actually dragons at all - they're bat, and nothing more.
I hope.
— Ars Gouda, p. 169
Bat Dragon is an enemy that appears in specific stages after obtaining the  Green Eyes Vizard relic.
Initially the stages can be identified by observing the stage icons pulsating from normal to pitch black with only glowing eyes of Bat Dragon left visible. Once defeated, the pulsation no longer occurs.
The Bat Dragon's position in the  Milky Way Map is indicated by a [A sprite icon representing Question Mark Black with no link] black question mark in Mad Forest and Il Molise. Before the fight, the Bat Dragon is indicated through a black silhouette. Once engaged and the fight begins, the stage timer is stopped until the Bat Dragon has been defeated, akin to  Big Fuzz [DLC] and the following Ode to Castlevania stage bosses:  Beelzebub, Doppelganger, and  Legion.
Defeating one Bat Dragon unlocks the  Unearthly Bolt. If the player defeated two unique Bat Dragons, the player will be able to purchase the  Enemahs. If the player uses  Big Troubler to defeat a Bat Dragon while having the  Game Killer (0) in hand, they will unlock the  Stake to Your Heart (0) Darkana.
## Variants
## Bat Dragon
| | Bat Dragon | ID: EX_BOSS_BATDRAGON_1
| Found in: Mad Forest, Il Molise
| Skills: HP x Level
| Health1,000★ | Power20 | MSpeed110 | KB (max)0 (1) | Res-Fr2 | Res-KYes | Res-DBYes | Res-DFYes | Weak-Fi? (edit) | XP100
## Appearances
A Bat Dragon will only appear if the player obtained the  Green Eyes Vizard relic. Once they do so, its position in the  Milky Way Map will be indicated by a [A sprite icon representing Question Mark Black with no link] black question mark, making it an enemy whose appearance isn't based on the timer/waves, but rather through location. The Bat Dragon will be available from the beginning of a run and can be encountered at any time.
In Mad Forest, it is located ~1.5 tilesets northwest of the starting area.
In Il Molise, it is located 5-6 tilesets northeast of the starting area.
## Trivia
- In addition to having the HP x Level ability, Bat Dragons have a unique minimum HP scaling level of 80.
- In practice, this means the Bat Dragon's HP only starts being multiplied by the player level upon it reaching level 81. No levels gained before reaching level 81 have an effect on the Bat Dragon's HP.
- Bat Dragon is most likely a reference to Bad Dragon, an "Adult toy" manufacturer.
- The color scheme of Bat Dragon is near-identical to Duke, the mascot of said manufacturer.

Beelzebub is a special boss enemy that appears in Ode to Castlevania. It is located in the room on the farthest northwestern corner of the castle, beyond a hallway immediately to the left of the room containing the  Silver Ring. However, Beelzebub cannot be fought and the door containing it cannot be opened until all the gate keys, including the  Serpent Gate and  Beast Gate, are obtained. 
Upon defeating Beelzebub for the first time, it will drop the  Pile of Secrets. On subsequent defeats, it will drop a  Treasure Chest containing five weapons or passive items.

“
Colossal skeletal protector of illicit Red Falcon munitions factories the world over. Near immune to small-arms fire and capable of ripping apart a tank with its bare hands, Big Fuzz is also considered an expert in industrial relations and complex labour legal manoeuvrings, and is thus widely respected by the factory workers.

Big Fuzz is a boss that can be encountered at the end of Neo Galuga during a special event that can be accessed after 27:00. The player has to step on to a red trap plate on the far top right of the city area to encounter him as a boss. Doing so will avoid having to encounter  The Reaper.
Defeating it will unlock Colonel Bahamut.

Despite appearances, these shambling cadavers change their bandages daily. Whatever’s going on underneath all those wrappings remains a mystery, but one presumes it’s thoroughly disgusting.
— Ars Gouda, p. 36
Big Mummy is an enemy primarily encountered in Mad Forest, with additional appearances in Inlaid Library and Boss Rash.
In Mad Forest, it appears as both a normal enemy and a boss. In Inlaid Library and Boss Rash, it appears exclusively as a boss.
Big Mummy is semi-resistant to knockback. Its boss variant has health that scales with the player's level and is partially resistant to freeze.

Boon Marrabbio is a special enemy that appears in Mad Forest. Defeating Boon Marrabbio for the first time unlocks him as a playable character.
Unlike most enemies, Boon Marrabbio does not spawn as a part a wave or map event, but spawns under certain conditions in Mad Forest after obtaining Yellow Sign.
Boon Marrabbio is resistant to knockback, instant kill effects, such as Pentagram and Rosary, and debuffs.

| {"stages":"Red Moon Manor","update":"Legacy of the Bloodmoon is out now!","dlc":"bm","resistances":"Freeze, Rosary","image":"","health":"70 - 100,000","movespeed":"120 - 160","release":"28 August 2026","skills":"-","name":"Congregation","damage":"5 - 20"}
← Jaman JatoCongregationAshtart →
“
A shapeless mass of Red Moon disciples, Congregation was born from a ritual gone wrong. 
Thirteen bodies were welded together, but only seven of the original souls remained conscious, tormented by peretual, excruciating pain. The remaining six allegedly got lucky: they died during the ritual.
The truth, unfortunately, is that the dead ones occasionally come back to life, just long enough to relive the horror of their physical form and feel its deadly, relentless pain. They bring with them terrifying memories from the hells their souls have passed through.
Before dying again, they share those hellish visions with the rest, leaving the living souls in anguish and desperation, knowing that not even death will end their suffering.
— Ars Gouda, p. Bm 17
Congregation is an enemy that appears in Red Moon Manor. Congregation appears on the west side of the manor.
It appears on the west side of the manor, the area is a room with a square swirl pattern that gets progressively smaller and is to the north-west of the Velvet Dodecahedron. Once getting close enough, it will automatically appear.

← Lord GhostCosmic EggSka'sa Ka'sos →
“
When the great many-faced god of all things encounrered the empty void, the void spoke thus, "Canst thou provide substance? Canst thou provide form?" And the many-faced god answered thus, "As a tonic for thy formless sorrow, I shall create a beginning of things. An embryonic gift, thinly shelled in the atomic matter of a new universe, and containing a concentration of reality." In modern english: "Can I offer you a cosmic egg in this trying time?"
— Ars Gouda, p. 138
The Cosmic Egg is an enemy that appears in Astral Stair. It is unique in that it intermittently casts a large, circular rainbow-colored pulse identical to that of the  Infinite Corridor, which also causes the player character's health to be halved if they are inside of its radius. This makes Cosmic Eggs dangerous enemies to defeat regardless of the player's build.
The Cosmic Egg's health is based on the player's level. It takes reduced knockback and is resistant to, freeze, debuffs, and instant kill.

# Death (boss)
This article is about the enemy. For the character, see Death (character).
DLC Content (Ode to Castlevania)
This article primarily features content only available in DLC, which may not be available in all copies of the game.
| Death [DLC]
| 
| Bestiary ?Enemy stats as displayed in the Bestiary.See individual variants for more detailed information.
| Release | ? (edit)
| Theme | "Dance of Illusions"
| Bestiary # | Oc 51
| HP | 3,000
| Power | 30
| Speed | 160
| Resistances | Freeze, Rosary, Debuff
| Skills | ? (edit)
| Found in | Ode to Castlevania
| {"type":"Normal","resistances":"Freeze, Rosary, Debuff","name":"Death","dlc":"oc","notes":"","image":"Sprite-Death (boss).png","stages":"Ode to Castlevania","movespeed":"160","health":"3,000","skills":"","theme":"Dance of Illusions (Harmony of Despair)","damage":"30"}
← ParanoiaDeathDoppelganger →
“
From world known to worlds most frigid, from fabled lakes to the depths of space, even to the far-flung future, one truth remains immutable in every reality we discover. This truth's name is Death. Here though, in the Castle, even Death himself serves another. His loyalty is admirable, and its implications are terrifying.
— Ars Gouda, p. Oc 51
Death is a special boss enemy that appears in Ode to Castlevania, and is the final boss of the Ode to Castlevania expansion.
## Behavior
Entering Ode to Castlevania with  Richter Belmont reveals a new boss summoning circle icon in the map, located in Dracula's Throne Room at the top left of the Clock Tower. Upon entering the room, enemies will cease to spawn unlike with other characters. Upon going up the stairs and entering the throne room, a dialogue between Richter and Dracula begins. With the help of  The Directer,  Vlad Tepes Dracula is put into a coffin for the player to interact with, thus being unlocked as a playable character, and which enrages Death. After a brief monologue, the fight against Death begins.
## Phase 1
After a few seconds, Death strikes the player with his scythe, replacing all of their weapons with a single  Whip. Death starts striking the player with his scythe, this time instantly killing them, however they are immediately revived by The Directer. After dying once, The Directer will appear in the bottom right corner of the screen seemingly charging an attack, and after dying twice, The Directer will try to stop Death's scythe with his hand, which leads to it being destroyed. The sound of a church bell tolls two times, before the screen freezes and the background becomes replaced by SMPTE color bars while a loud glitch sound is played, followed by The Directer shattering into fragments and beginning Phase 2.
## Phase 2
The player now cannot move for a short period before the  Aspect of Seawinds homes into the player, allowing them to move and interact with things. However, the player can now die if they touch Death despite not having a health bar yet and will not be revived by  The Directer this time. Shortly after, the  Aspect of Sun appears and is collected by the player, giving them a visible health bar. Then the  Aspect of Volcano is pulled to the player, making the Whip begin to attack again. This is then followed by the  Aspect of Moon, bringing back the music and making  Experience Gems show up at the very bottom of the screen, however the player can only accumulate XP and level up to regain their weapons once they obtain the  Aspect of City, that comes a few seconds after. Finally, the  Aspect of Stone appears a few seconds afterwards, before the  Aspect of Black is collected.
After the Aspect of Black is collected, the music cuts off and it appears in the center of the screen, before slowly breaking apart to reveal the  Black Disk. The Black Disk starts spinning as the famous hidden warning message from Alucard from Castlevania: Symphony of the Night begins playing: "As you can see, this is a black disk. Cut number 1 contains computer data, so please, don't play it. But you probably won't listen to me anyway, will you?" This is then followed by the bonus track version of the song Dracula's Castle, starting Phase 3.
## Phase 3
All base characters from Ode to Castlevania start to appear to help Richter in the chronological order they appear in the original Castlevania Series Timeline, and are then followed by the base roster of Vampire Survivors as well as Dracula himself, the order being the following:
-  Leon Belmont
-  Sonia Belmont
-  Trevor Belmont,  Sypha Belnades and  Grant Danasty
-  Alucard and  Hector
-  Christopher Belmont
-  Simon Belmont
-  Juste Belmont
-  Maria Renard
-  Shanoa
-  John Morris and  Eric Lecarde
-  Jonathan Morris and  Charlotte Aulin
-  Soma Cruz,  Yoko Belnades and  Julius Belmont
-  Antonio Belpaese,  Imelda Belpaese,  Pasqualina Belpaese,  Gennaro Belpaese and  Zi'Assunta Belpaese
-  Arca Ladonna,  Porta Ladonna,  Lama Ladonna and  Poe Ratcho
-  Suor Clerici,  Dommario,  Krochi Freetto and  Christine Davain
-  Pugnala Provola,  Giovanna Grana,  Poppea Pecorina and  Concetta Caciotta
-  Mortaccio,  Yatta Cavallo,  Bianca Ramba and  O'Sole Meeo
-  Vlad Tepes Dracula
Defeating Death and viewing the ending credits for the first time unlocks all the 20 hidden Ode to Castlevania characters and the rest of the Ode to Castlevania stage.

## Trivia
- Death's boss fight is one of three times that a stage victory will not end with the player's death, with the other two being Holy Forbidden and  The Directer's boss fight.
- The theme that plays when this boss is encountered is Dance of Illusions.
- Death replacing the player's weapons and passive items with a  Whip is a reference to the cutscene when he first appears in Castlevania: Symphony of the Night, in which he steals Alucard's equipment and scatters it across Dracula's Castle.
- The jingle that plays after Death is defeated is the stage clear jingle from Castlevania: Bloodlines.

| {"image":"Sprite-Diamond.png","stages":"Bat Country","movespeed":"0 – 300","health":"50","damage":"10 – 30"}
Diamonds are event enemies that appear in Bat Country. They appear periodically throughout the stage in Monster Dance, Diamond Road, Diamond Square and Diamond Maze events, sometimes bouncing up from the bottom of the screen and other times appearing in various formations around the player.
Diamonds take no knockback and usually do not move, unless they are bouncing upwards from the bottom of the screen. Upon spawning, Diamonds are blue. Each time they get hit, they change color, turning green, then yellow, then pink. After getting hit four times, they die. When they are killed, they drop coins and pickups, similarly to a light source.

| {"update":"Patch 0.4.2","release":"5 April 2022","image":"Sprite-Dragon Shrimp.png","stages":"Gallo Tower","movespeed":"200 – 400","caption":"","skills":"HP x Level, Fixed Direction","health":"4 – 20","damage":"3 – 8"}
← Milk ElementalDragon ShrimpSig.ra Rossi →
“
When a shrimp yearns to be a dragon above all things, the Horned Shrimp may take pity on it and bestow a form most mighty. Or rather, a form almost mighty. It turns out that being the patron god of all shrimp doesn’t let you make anything much more intimidating than this, but the shrimp are happy!
— Ars Gouda, p. 16
Dragon Shrimp is an enemy that appears in Gallo Tower, Boss Rash and The Bone Zone. It first appears as an map event enemy and later also as a normal enemy and a boss. The "flag" variants appear in the Dragon Stream, Dragon Swarm, and Skeleton Stream events, whereas the regular variants spawn periodically during waves. The boss variant appears both as a normal and boss enemy.
Killing 3,000 Dragon Shrimps is the requirement for unlocking  O'Sole Meeo. This count does not include the unlisted Dragon Shrimp variants.
Dragon Shrimp's health is based on the player's level and it takes reduced knockback. The flag variant's movement is fixed on horizontal axis.
## Behavior
While non-flag variants of the Dragon Shrimp have the same behavior as most enemies, which tend to gravitate towards the player's character and damage them upon touching the character, the flag variants tend to make horizontal, zig-zag movements that do not follow the player's character. Flag variant Dragon Shrimps can only deal damage to the player character through their heads, and likewise, the player character can only deal damage to, freeze, or defang this enemy by targeting its head.
## Variants
## Dragon Shrimp 1
| | Dragon Shrimp 1 | ID: XLDRAGON1

## Tips
- Players can safely cross through the body of the flag variants of the Dragon Shrimp, since it will not deal damage. However, the only part that can deal damage to the player is its head.
- Likewise, the player cannot deal damage to the body either, they can only attack flag variants of the Dragon Shrimp on its head.
## Trivia
- Dragon Shrimp 1 (boss) has the same statline as the boss variants of Musc Musc, Harzia, Lionhead, Panther, and Dust Elemental.
- Dragon Shrimp bear a resemblance to the White Dragon enemy from several games in the Castlevania series.
- In addition to resembling the White Dragon, the old sprite for the Dragon Shrimp uncannily resembles the head of the Bone Dragon King, a boss from Castlevania III: Dracula's Curse.
- They also look very similar to Bundles from the Contra series.
- When a flag variant of a Dragon Shrimp freezes, only its head will get frozen while the rest of its body won't. However, its body will continually shorten towards the frozen head.

← WerewolfDust ElementalLionhead →
“
If knowledge is not known, it ceases to be knowledge. The Inlaid Library’s virulent swell of forbidden lore yearns to inscribe itself into an open mind. Left for decades with only dust for company, that lore made a home for itself. Fortunate are we that the Dust Elementals have no tongue to speak it.

Dust Elemental is an enemy that appears in Inlaid Library, Laborratory, Boss Rash, and Eudaimonia Machine. In Inlaid Library and Boss Rash, it appears both as a normal enemy and as a boss. In Laborratory, it appears as a normal enemy and as parts of map events. In Eudaimonia Machine, it is summoned by The Directer.
Its boss variant's health is based on the player's level.

← Space PickleECMASlimeSinistronz →
“
These blobs are actually super friendly and just want to deliver you a pleasant Experience. Unfortunately, they aren't aware that their bodies are composed of a slightly acidic slime.
— Ars Gouda, p. 86
ECMASlime is an enemy that appears in Space 54.
## Behavior
Just like most enemies, they tend to gravitate towards the player's character and deal damage if they touch the character. However, a unique trait among larger variants of the ECMASlimes is that they spawn in smaller variants once they get killed. Extra large variants spawn in 2 large variants upon their death, while the large ones spawn in 2 normal-sized ones upon their death.

# Enemies
Enemies
A variety of enemies in the game.
From left to right: The Directer (bottom left corner), The Reaper, Glowing Bat, Stage Killer, Megalo Impostor Rina, Je-Ne-Viv, Moon Duck.
Enemies are entities in Vampire Survivors that attack the player's character, simply by approaching them. By killing enemies the player may gain  experience gems and can level up, gaining access to more weapons and passives. A list of all enemies, along with their stats and descriptions, can be found in the game's Bestiary.
## Spawning
Upon entering a stage, an initial amount of enemies depending on the stage modifiers spawns. Thereafter the game periodically attempts to spawn enemies. Enemies generally spawn just outside the screen and can despawn if the player moves far enough from them. 
## Waves
Enemies normally arrive in waves - one wave every minute. Each wave specifies a minimum amount and a spawn interval for the enemies. If the minimum amount is not met when the game attempts to spawn enemies, enemies will be spawned until the quota is filled. If more enemies than the minimum amount is present, one of each type of enemies in the wave will be spawned. When 300 or more enemies are alive the game will not spawn more enemies periodically, and only bosses and enemies from map events can spawn.
## Bosses
Bosses are special enemies that spawn in some waves. They are stronger than other enemies in the wave, having more health, dealing more damage and often also being resistant to some effects. Bosses also have a chance to drop a  Treasure Chest when they are killed. Bosses do not despawn when the player moves away from them, but get teleported back to the screen.
## Map events
Map events are short events that spawn groups of enemies outside the regular spawning cycle. Map events can, for example, summon a swarm of enemies that quickly sweep across the screen or encircle the player with high health enemies. 
## Wave events
Map events are usually tied to a wave and trigger at the same second marks every time for that wave, making it possible to anticipate them. Multiple events can take place during a wave. Each event specifies how many enemies are spawned, how many times and what intervals the event is repeated.
Wave events usually also have a chance for them to happen, which is reduced by  Luck:
chanceWithLuck=eventChancetotalLuck
The chance is unique for each event and is evaluated for every repeat of the event. If the event has no chance listed or a chance of 0, it is guaranteed to play out.
## Traps
Dairy Plant and Gallo Tower feature circular pressure plates on the ground, which upon being stepped on trigger an unavoidable event. The event chosen at random from a set of predetermined options unique to the stage. The trap events have a global cooldown, which depends on Luck:
effectiveCooldown=trapCooldown⋅totalLuck
## Special events
Special events are usually one time events that are spawned on global timer and/or have unique trigger conditions. They are not part of the stage's waves, so the enemies introduced through them cannot appear in Green Acres.
## Stats
Like the player, enemies have stats, which define how they interact with other effects.
## Base stats
Health
Health describes how much damage is required to kill the enemy.
Power
Power describes how much damage the enemy does to the player upon coming in contact with them before modifications, such as Armor.
Speed
Speed, or MoveSpeed, describes how fast the enemy moves.
Knockback
Knockback is a multiplier for the strength of the pushing effect when hit by the player's weapons.
XP
XP describes how much experience the enemy grants when killed.
## Resistances
Freeze resistance
Freeze resistance prevents the enemy from being frozen by the player. It is given as a numerical value. If the enemy's freeze resistance is higher than freeze chance of the weapon they were hit by it will not be frozen. Freeze from Orologion goes through freeze resistance.
Instant kill resistance
Instant kill resistance grants immunity to effects that instantly defeat an enemy by dealing damage to them equal to their maximum health. Instant kill can be applied by Pentagram, Gorgeous Moon and Rosary.
Debuff resistance
Debuff resistance grants an immunity to effects that weaken the affected enemy upon hit. Effects classified as debuffs are the knockback and freeze resistance reductions from Garlic and Soul Eater, and the slow effect from Mannajja.
## Skills
Skills are passive effects some enemies have.
HP x Level
HP x Level multiplies the enemy's health based on the player's level. This is applied the moment the enemy was spawned, and will not be updated in case the player gains levels while it's alive.
Fixed Direction
Causes the enemy to only move in a straight line, instead of continuously homing to the player.
Floaty
Also known as "Medusa". Causes the enemy to move in a wavy pattern.
## Effects
Some mechanics in the game specifically target enemies, altering their stats and spawning. 
## Curse
Main article: Curse
Curse is a stat that increases the Max Health and Move Speed of the enemies and the frequency they spawn. The new spawn interval with any amount of Curse can be calculated as follows:
effectiveSpawnInterval=spawnIntervaltotalCurse
## Hyper mode
Main article: Stage § Modes
Hyper mode in each stage increases the minimum amount of enemies spawned and the movement speed of enemies. It may also increase their maximum health.

## List of enemies
Currently, there are 392 unique bestiary entries in the game. Any enemy that has a yellow name in the [Ars Gouda] Bestiary is required for an unlock. For example, [Dragon Shrimp] has a yellow name because the player must kill 3000 for  O'Sole Meeo.

| {"type":"Normal","dlc":"ed","resistances":"","image":"Sprite-Failinis.png","health":"80 - 220","movespeed":"200 - 230","notes":"","skills":"","stages":"Emerald Diorama","damage":"9 - 15"}
← DecobatFailinisRana Combatant →
“
Dangerous dog-beasts believed to originate from Pulchra are usually called either Failinis or Barghests, though they're clearly different colours and have very little else in common. Barghest specimens studied in Delta Base are well known by analysts for their propensity to tear anything left unattended to shreds with their fearsome fangs, and for tearing up the discotheque with their Azure Dance.
Failinis, on the other hand, once were genies who became infernal meanies. They love to eat paninis and get wasted on martinis. As demons go they're teeny and they look cute in a beanie, but don't assume they're weenies, they still tempted Paganini. These claims were made by a somewhat wobbly citizen after they were unfrozen in Grelon, so they might not be entirely accurate.
— Ars Gouda, p. Ed 10
Failinis is an enemy that appears in Emerald Diorama.

Fake Fallen Cherub is an enemy that appears in Cappella Magna and Holy Forbidden. In Cappella Magna, it only appears as a normal enemy. In Holy Forbidden, it appears both as a normal enemy and as a special enemy spawned by The Maddener.
Fake Fallen Cherub originally looks like a bat but takes an angelic form in Maddener's presence.

| {"image":"Sprite-Fake Fallen Cherubbello.png","stages":"Cappella Magna","movespeed":"100 – 330","release":"23 May 2022","health":"11 – 50","update":"Patch 0.6.1","damage":"3 – 11"}
Fake Fallen Cherubbello is an enemy that appears in Cappella Magna and Holy Forbidden. In Cappella Magna, it first appears as a boss and later also as a normal enemy. In Holy Forbidden, it appears as a special enemy spawned by The Maddener.
Fake Fallen Cherubbello originally looks like a bat, but changes appearance in Maddener's presence. The normal variant takes reduced knockback. The boss variant's health is based on the player's level. The variant in Holy Forbidden has a self-destruct attack, which triggers immediately after spawning. After 2 seconds it explodes dealing 50 damage.

A mage from a distant realm swore its cold face resembled a great scholar who abandoned her tower and disappeared without a trace. He boldly dared to speak with the creature, and did so for hours. None dared intervene, even as the night air froze and the mage, transfixed, froze with it. Witnesses say he uttered one last thing as the tears in his eyes turned to ice: “She is alive there.”
— Ars Gouda, p. Fs 32
Followa is an enemy that appears in Abyss Foscari.

Death's demonic servant has only three desires: spicy food, a horrible end to the enemies of the grim reaper, and to hang out with Slogra.

Gaibon is an enemy that appears in Ode to Castlevania. This enemy spawns in the western part of the castle, in the corridor to the left of the first portal. This appears when you press the plate with the skull symbol.
Once defeated, the plate reappears, and pressing it a second time will spawn Slogra. Defeating Gaibon and Slogra with  Grant Danasty unlocks  Sypha Belnades.

This enemy does not have a bestiary entry. The name given to it by the wiki is based on its characteristics and may not be canonical.
| Gallo

| HP | 200★
| Power | 30
| Speed | 160
| Resistances | Freeze, Instant kill, Debuff, Knockback
| Skills | HP x Level
| Found in | Gallo Tower
| {"update":"Patch 0.4.2","name":"Gallo","movespeed":"160","image":"Sprite-Gallo (enemy).png","stages":"Gallo Tower","resistances":"Freeze, Instant kill, Debuff, Knockback","release":"5 April 2022","skills":"HP x Level","health":"200","damage":"30"}
Gallo is an enemy that appears in Gallo Tower. It only appears as a boss and has a chance to drop a Treasure Chest.
Gallo's health is based on the player's level. It takes reduced knockback and is immune to freeze, instant kill effects and debuffs. Gallo shoots bullets every 0.5 seconds and also has a rotating beam attack, resembling Clock Lancet. It does not deal damage but briefly slows the player upon hit.
There was a Garlic-related bug in a past (1.8.208) version of the game, where Gallo would be knocked back towards the player rather than away when taking damage, resulting in a quick death very reminiscent of the encounter with The Reaper.

← ManticoreGallotriceBig Golem →
“
Gallo had long been haunted by frequent nightmares of being petrified slowly from the feet up to the head, but perhaps more frightening were his dreams of a gigantic predatory chicken. The Gallotrice’s birth was the moment he began to consider that scientific blasphemy as a form of emotional therapy might not be helping anybody.
— Ars Gouda, p. 54
Gallotrice is an enemy that appears in Dairy Plant, Gallo Tower, Tiny Bridge, Boss Rash, and Lake Foscari [DLC]. It has a normal variant, which only appears as a normal enemy in Dairy Plant and Gallo Tower, and a boss variant, which appears as a boss in Dairy Plant, Gallo Tower and Boss Rash. The gold variant only appears in Tiny Bridge as a normal enemy.
Gallotrice takes reduced knockback. Its boss variant is also resistant to freeze, instant kill and debuffs, and its health is based on the player's level.

## Trivia
- Gallotrice, excluding its gold variant, has the same statline as Manticore, which appears in Gallo Tower.
- Gallotrice's name is a play on words using the words 'cockatrice' and the Italian word for a rooster/cock, 'gallo'.

| {"caption":"","image":"Sprite-Ghost.png","stages":"Mad Forest, Inlaid Library, Gallo Tower","movespeed":"200","release":"27 October 2021","health":"10","update":"There exist tales that don't exist.","damage":"5"}
← SkelegemGhostWerewolf →
“
A pure fabrication, the idle fantasy of brains gripped with fever or boredom. There’s simply no such thing as ghosts, and the mere suggestion is to be mocked as ridiculous. These particular apparitions are probably just a trick of the light. Tricks of the light can have hit points too!
— Ars Gouda, p. 11
Ghost is an enemy primarily encountered in Mad Forest, Inlaid Library, and Gallo Tower, with additional appearances in Moongolow, Whiteout, Astral Stair, Tiny Bridge, and Eudaimonia Machine.
In Inlaid Library, Gallo Tower, Moongolow, Whiteout, Astral Stair, and Tiny Bridge, it appears exclusively as normal enemies. In Mad Forest, it spawns both as a normal enemy and during Ghost Swarm map events. In Boss Rash, it appears only during Ghost Swarm events. In Eudaimonia Machine, it is summoned by The Directer as a special enemy during the second phase of the stage.
Ghost is semi-resistant to knockback, and the Swarm variant only moves in a fixed direction.

This article is about the Base Game enemy. For the DLC enemy, see Giant Bat (boss).
| Giant Bat

| Release | 27 October 2021 (Update)

| {"image":"Sprite-Giant Bat.png","stages":"Mad Forest","movespeed":"140","release":"27 October 2021","health":"270","update":"There exist tales that don't exist.","damage":"10"}
← MerdusaGiant BatMantichana →
“
Wreathed in shadow, eyes glowing red, such oversized terrors that flap in the night must surely be the sign of a vampire’s approach, yes? Well, no. They’re bats, and nothing more. I’ll admit they’re really bloody big, though.
— Ars Gouda, p. 34
Giant Bat is a normal enemy primarily encountered in Mad Forest, with additional appearances in Inlaid Library, Boss Rash, Tiny Bridge, and Bat Country.
In Mad Forest, it first appears as a boss and later reappears as a normal enemy in later waves. In Inlaid Library, Bat Country, and Tiny Bridge, it appears only as a normal enemy. In Boss Rash, it appears exclusively as a boss.
Giant Bat is semi-resistant to knockback and features a partial hitbox: the upper portion of its wings lacks collision, allowing it to partially pass through walls and other enemies without obstruction.

# Giant Enemy Crab
| Giant Enemy Crab
| 
| Bestiary ?Enemy stats as displayed in the Bestiary.See individual variants for more detailed information.
| Release | 5 April 2022 (Update)

| Skills | HP x Level
| Found in | Gallo Tower
| {"update":"Patch 0.4.2","stages":"Gallo Tower","image":"Sprite-Giant Enemy Crab.png","health":"500","resistances":"Freeze, Instant kill, Debuffs","release":"5 April 2022","skills":"HP x Level","movespeed":"200","damage":"30"}
← Sword GuardianGiant Enemy CrabSad Molisano →
“
With a name like theirs, these colossal crustaceans were kind of railroaded into antagonism by a society that didn’t want to understand them. Would it surprise you to learn that Giant Enemy Crabs have an entire culture of their own? Schools of philosophy? Arts and education? Bigotry truly is a sad, sad thing.
— Ars Gouda, p. 58
Giant Enemy Crab is an enemy that appears in Gallo Tower and Boss Rash. It only appears as a boss and has a chance to drop a Treasure Chest. Defeating Giant Enemy Crab in Gallo Tower is the requirement for unlocking the stage's hyper mode.
## Behavior
Giant Enemy Crab's health is based on the player's level and it takes reduced knockback. It is also resistant to freeze, instant kill and debuffs. Giant Enemy Crab has 2 Pincers attached to it, which grow upon receiving damage and can regenerate a short while after being destroyed, up to 17 times.
In Gallo Tower, it can also summon a Drowner, if the player spends longer than 6 seconds below it, and the chest dropped by it can exceptionally evolve up to 5 weapons, provided that a higher tier chest with enough rewards was received.
In Boss Rash, Giant Enemy Crab has slightly lower health and speed.

← Alien CentipedeGulcan TankMetal Alien →
“
By combining the fearsome firepower of the High Speed Tank Booby with the bipedal versatility of the Buried Surfacing Cannon Gulcan, Red Falcon's traitor engineers have created a war machine that is somehow less than the sum of its parts in every conceivable way. Prone to tumbling over due to its top-heavy design and unable to pivot its main turret up or down, the pilots of these botched battle vehicles rely on clumsily stomping their enemies underfoot as the most reliable means of victory. Every member of Red Falcon does, however, remark on how cool they look and how badass it feels to drive one of these horrendous Frankenstein's hot-rods.
— Ars Gouda, p. Og 6
Gulcan Tank is an enemy that appears in Neo Galuga. It first appears as a boss before making an appearance as a normal enemy.

← KizzuneHoly Circuit CreationsBounty Hunter →
“
Santa Ladonna's experimental alchemy often has unforeseen side-effects, such as these strange automatons. When one of Ladonna's rituals went awry, she accidentally opened an angelic gate that spewed celestial magic onto a pile of her old VHS tapes, spawning this clanking cadre of retro robots.
— Ars Gouda, p. 69
Holy Circuit Creations are a group of enemies that appear in Laborratory and Carlo Cart. They appear as normal enemies, bosses, and as parts of map events.
This group consists of four distinct units, each with unique characteristics, which are as follows: ConstableBot, RetroBot, T-DCCC, and ER-2000.

## Trivia
- The Holy Circuit Creations are direct references to various characters from popular classic films of the science-fiction genre. Each unit's reference is what follows:
- RetroBot is a reference to Number 5, also nicknamed "Johnny", the main character of the Short Circuit films.
- T-DCCC is a reference to T-800, also known as "The Terminator", the name of several characters from the Terminator franchise. To note, 'DCCC' is the Roman numeral form of the number 800.
- ConstableBot is a reference to RoboCop, the main protagonist of the RoboCop franchise.
- ER-2000 is a reference to ED-209, a fictional armed robot that also appears in the RoboCop franchise, which serves as minor antagonists and adversaries to the titular main character.

← Metal AlienHuman Faced DogGarth →
“
When the Aliens first made contact they struggled to overcome humanity's innate distruct of the other, making brainwashing more difficult. Initially they began bio-engineering a pseudo-human to act as their mouthpiece, but scrapped the idea as humans didn't seem to particularly trust other humans either. Here we have attempt number two, inspired by humanity's near universal trust of canines. The human face was a design misstep.
— Ars Gouda, p. Og 8

These quasi-angelic spirits are thought to be manifestations of maleficent thoughts, born to constrain others with their Freezing Blaster. — Ars Gouda, p. Ed 21

A being made of particularly dusty dust, it is the ancient enemy of the twin deities Shaik and Vack. So goes one myth. Another says they come from the future, their dust being the ashes of those whose death they've come to show. Actually, that's a really good idea, I should be saving it for a sci-fi novella.
Dustlords of Tomorrow: Book One of the Dusts of Future Dust Trilogy, available wherever books aren't yet being burned for fuel.
— Ars Gouda, p. Bm 16
Jaman Jato is an enemy that appears in Red Moon Manor.

# Je-Ne-Viv (enemy)
This article is about the boss. For the character, see Je-Ne-Viv.
DLC Content (Tides of the Foscari)
This article primarily features content only available in DLC, which may not be available in all copies of the game.
| Je-Ne-Viv [DLC]
| 
| Bestiary ?Enemy stats as displayed in the Bestiary.See individual variants for more detailed information.
| Release | 13 April 2023 (Update)
| Theme | "The World Eater"
| Bestiary # | Fs 42
| HP | 3,000★
| Power | 40
| Speed | 170
| Resistances | Freeze, Debuff, Rosary
| Skills | HP x Level
| Found in | Abyss Foscari
| {"stages":"Abyss Foscari","name":"Je-Ne-Viv","update":"New DLC & Patch 1.4.0","dlc":"tf","resistances":"Freeze, Debuff, Rosary","image":"Sprite-Je-Ne-Viv.png","health":"3,000","movespeed":"170","release":"13 April 2023","skills":"HP x Level","theme":"The World Eater (track)","damage":"40"}
← FomoriansJe-Ne-VivFrijjitello →
“
The dread devourer, the grand consumer, the eater of worlds. It has tasted all that creation has to offer, and all of creation is reflected in its ravening form. It is part human. Part monster. Part undead. Part demon. Part abomination. It is not a vampire. It is yet to meet a vampire. It would very much like to eat a vampire. It wants to know if you've seen any vampires. It will be far too busy eating you and everything around you to ask.
— Ars Gouda, p. Fs 42
Je-Ne-Viv is a special boss enemy that appears in Abyss Foscari, and is the final boss of the Tides of the Foscari expansion.
Defeating Je-Ne-Viv in Abyss Foscari unlocks the stage's hyper mode.
Je-Ne-Viv's health is based on the player's level. It takes reduced knockback and is resistant to, freeze, debuffs, and instant kill.
## Behavior
Je-Ne-Viv can only be encountered after Abyss Foscari has been unlocked. In order to fight this boss, the second half of the map must be unlocked by destroying the relevant crystal with  Maruto Cuts and his evolved weapon,  Legionnaire. Subsequently, the player must enter the previously darkened section of the map with  Eleanor Uziron and reach the crystal Je-Ne-Viv is sealed in. If Eleanor has the final evolved form of her base weapon, the  SpellStrom, the crystal will eventually break and the battle against Je-Ne-Viv will begin. Be warned that approaching too close to the crystal, even while Je-Ne-Viv is still sealed, causes contact damage.
## Phase 1
Once the crystal sealing Je-Ne-Viv is destroyed, the track "The World Eater" begins playing and the game cannot be paused. Later, all of the current character's  Golden Egg bonuses will be cleared, and three waves of two rings of 50  Shield Sneks spawn with a screen shake, which then converge on Eleanor. Each wave is about 7 seconds apart. After a while, Je-Ne-Viv breaks free and gains 10,000 more health. Shortly after, it starts chasing Eleanor at a tenth of its speed and the camera permanently locks onto Je-Ne-Viv. Another 3.5 seconds passes, it slowly regaining its colors and moves at normal speed. The player needs to avoid it, as it will deal massive damage on contact.
After 10 seconds of chasing, Je-Ne-Viv will stand in place and charge up power before using World Eater, then moves at half speed and devouring Eleanor's power, but also spawn the  Whip and  Magic Wand on the stage. At this point, Je-Ne-Viv's sprite will incorporate a simulacrum of Eleanor's, while Eleanor's own sprite will become bleak and discolored, and she will immediately lose all her weapons (but keep passive items and counterparts). Dozens of  Little Hearts will detach from Eleanor and flow into Je-Ne-Viv, healing it as it grows to immense proportions. Once this process is complete, the true battle begins.
## Phase 2
Je-Ne-Viv will continue chasing after Eleanor at normal speed (1.2x in hyper mode), dealing massive damage on contact. In addition to this, it has two other main forms of attack:
- It will periodically spawn 2-10  Ophion zones every 8.353 seconds, the location of which is outlined by red circles before they appear. The first blast consist of 13 zones.
- It will continuously spawn 6 Shield Sneks and 6  Bomb Sneks every 6.701 seconds. The Bomb Sneks will chase after Eleanor and attempt to explode, much like the  Sig.ra Rossis in Inlaid Library, and are killable. The first wave spawns 12 Shield Sneks and 24 Bomb Sneks.
Experience in this section is nearly non-existent. To fight back against Je-Ne-Viv, Eleanor must collect weapons scattered around the area, which will make it easier to fend off Je-Ne-Viv's Sneks. 5 seconds after Je-Ne-Viv reaches full power, a  Prismatic Missile spawns, and every 10 seconds after that. Pickups of Prismatic Missile will continue spawning in the area until Eleanor has maxed out the weapon, at which point  Crowns will start spawning if Eleanor does not have one already. Once both items have been maxed out, a  Treasure Chest will spawn shortly afterwards, evolving the Prismatic Missile into  Luminaire.
## Phase 3
Once Luminaire has been acquired, the music will change to "The Heart of the World" as  Luminaire Foscari herself flies to the top of the screen to assist Eleanor and replaces the in-game timer. Luminaire Foscari showers Je-Ne-Viv with heavenly, rainbow-colored beams, which deal disproportionate damage to Je-Ne-Viv compared to other weapons (in the thousands), effectively representing the only realistic way to end the fight.
This does not have to happen though, and it is technically possible to defeat Je-Ne-Viv before getting the Luminaire. This will end up resulting in a glitch where the prismatic missiles keep spawning. If Eleanor has already fully upgraded the prismatic missile, and then picks up another, it will cause yet another glitch. It destroys all other weapons Elenor is using and resets the level of the held prismatic missile back to level one. At around the time of the fifth glitched spawn, Elenor will die to the  White Hand.
If the player completes the battle normally and manages to survive long enough, Je-Ne-Viv will finally meet its end, emitting an echoing growl six times before disintegrating into ashes.
As the bell strikes twelve, signaling the end of the level, Eleanor's sprite will also slowly fade away into nothingness before the  White Hand can even reach her, implying that the battle took too much out of her to allow her to survive. This may also imply that, due to Eleanor having already been absorbed by Je-Ne-Viv, her physical form was also destroyed along with it, leaving her soul to slowly cease to exist without a body to inhabit.
## Other characters
After breaking the seal, other characters can fight Je-Ne-Viv by going to the former location of the Seal of the Banished. The fight immediately starts at the main phase and does not trigger anything special when it is defeated.
## Tips
- Leaving as many  Experience Gems as possible on the ground before the fight will be useful, as leveling up will provide new weapons for the fight.
- Counterparts will not be removed at the beginning of the boss fight, which means that collecting strong counterparts before the fight will help a lot.

This gives the Magic Wand (provided you pick it up first) that spawns a ridiculously fast fire rate and can whittle down Je-Ne-Viv’s health before Luminaire is obtained. In conjunction, leveling up before picking up any of the spawned weapons will allow you to pick your own which will help contribute.

These undead creatures hail from China, yet claim to have met and battled the Dark Lord's very own son when he was a child. There is no record of Alucard ever visiting China in his childhood, so it's quite possible the Jiang Shi are just trying to kid Dracula.

Jiang Shi is an enemy that appears in Ode to Castlevania. Like all other Castlevania bosses, the summoning circle needs to be used, but access to Jiang Shi's circle is locked behind the  Black Disk. After obtaining the  Black Disk within the new portions unlocked, Jiang Shi's summoning circle is in the right-most building at the very top.
It drops  Refectio upon being defeated.

“
Have you ever read the tragedy of Seiu Darkasso the Sly? It's not a story many who fight the forces of hell would tell you. It's a vampire legend.
Seiu was a warlock thrall, one so mighty and sly that they could control blood through occult incantations, manipulating life itself. This warlock had such knowledge of hemomancy, they could even prevent death... though, yes, mostly they use these powers to kill people. Seiu became so mighty, they only thing they feared was losing their sanguinary power. Eventually, of course, they did. They taught their neophyte every dark art they knew. Then the neophyte killed them while they were taking a power nap. Isn't it ironic? Seiu could prevent death, even reverse it, but not for themself.
Anyway, Ju Darkasso is the neophyte that killed Seiu Darkasso. Honestly, the less said about this warlock weirdo, the better.
— Ars Gouda, p. 140
Juda R'kasso is an enemy that appears in Room 1665.

# Leda (enemy)
This article is about the enemy. For the playable character, see Leda.
This enemy does not have a bestiary entry. The name given to it by the wiki is based on its characteristics and may not be canonical.
| Leda
| 
| Enemy
| Release | ? (edit)
| Theme | No
| HP | 88,880
| Power | 50
| Speed | 100
| Resistances | Freeze, Instant kill, Debuff, Knockback
| Skills | ? (edit)
| Found in | Gallo Tower
| {"image":"Sprite-Leda (enemy).png","name":"Leda","resistances":"Freeze, Instant kill, Debuff, Knockback","stages":"Gallo Tower","movespeed":"100","health":"88,880","damage":"50"}
Leda is a special enemy that appears in Gallo Tower. Defeating Leda for the first time unlocks her as a playable character.
Unlike most enemies, Leda does not spawn as a part a wave or map event, but can be found naturally in Gallo Tower's lower parts, around 43-44 tilesets (map tiles) below the starting area. When approaching her location, the area beyond a certain radius from the player gradually darkens until it becomes black and symbols begin to appear and travel vertically in sine wave pattern. When the player is roughly 90% of the way to her location, the background music also changes. When Leda is killed, the darkness effect vanishes.
Leda has high health and deals high damage but is rather slow. She is immune to knockback, freeze, instant kill effects, such as Pentagram and Rosary, and debuffs.
## Variants
## Leda
| | Leda | ID: BOSS_XLLEDA
| Found in: Gallo Tower
| Skills: -
| Health88,880 | Power50 | MSpeed100 | KB (max)0 (0) | Res-Fr2 | Res-KYes | Res-DBYes | Res-DF? (edit) | Weak-Fi? (edit) | XP50
## Appearances
Leda
| Stage | Minute | Spawn Type | Notes
| Gallo Tower | - | Special | Leda is found deep in the basement of Gallo Tower.
## Tips
- It is recommended to use a character with high Movement Speed and Wings to battle Leda, as it may be impossible to reach and defeat her before the time limit with slower characters.
- Hurry mode is inadvisable since it cuts your available time in half.
- Hyper mode is a valid option, since it gives +75% movement speed (caps out at 90% stage bonus, see stage).
- Endless mode removes the time limit pressure.
- Poppea Pecorina can be used to reach this boss quickly by walking straight downwards while leveling up her Song of Mana.
- Mask of the Red Death has +100 movement speed and can reach Leda very easily.
- The Arcana Game Killer can be combined with an ample-leveled character (around level 50 so long as you can clear mobs easily) to kill this boss very quickly.

| Legion [DLC]
| 
| Enemy
| Release | ? (edit)

| HP | 400★
| Power | 30
| Speed | 160
| Resistances | Freeze, Rosary, Debuff, Infinite Corridor

| Found in | Ode to Castlevania
| {"type":"Normal","resistances":"Freeze, Rosary, Debuff, Infinite Corridor","name":"Legion","dlc":"oc","notes":"","image":"Sprite-Legion.png","stages":"Ode to Castlevania","movespeed":"160","health":"400","skills":"HP x Level","theme":"Festival Of Servants","damage":"30"}
Legion is a special boss enemy that appears in Ode to Castlevania. It is located in the room on the farthest southeastern corner of the castle, by following the hallway going to the left below the room containing  Dominus Anger and the summoning circle of the  Succubus. However, Legion cannot be fought and the door containing it cannot be opened until all the gate keys, including the  Serpent Gate and  Beast Gate, are obtained. 
Upon defeating Legion for the first time, it will drop the  Ebony and Crimson Stones. On subsequent defeats, it will drop a  Treasure Chest containing five weapons or passive items.
## Behavior
Upon entering Legion's boss room and triggering its boss encounter, all remaining enemies in the stage will be despawned and the in-game timer is paused. Legion then rises from the floor before floating in the center of the room, starting the fight.
Legion has a massive outer shell composed of nine separate sections, one for each of the cardinal and intercardinal directions as well as one in the center. Each part of its outer shell intermittently releases groups of corpses that fall and walk along the ground and damage the player upon contact, but are quite frail and easy to defeat. 
Depleting the health of a section of Legion's outer shell causes it to fall apart and reveal a part of its core, as well as a tentacle in the matching direction outside of the central section. If the central core is exposed, two of the tentacles closest to the direction of the player's location briefly open before firing a damaging green laser beam for about 3 seconds, and sprayed slightly to the tentacle's left and right. Upon the beginning the fight, only eight outer pieces of Legion's outer shell can be fought, and an outer piece must be defeated for the inner piece to be made vulnerable. Once all nine pieces of Legion's outer shell are destroyed, Legion's core can now be damaged by the player.
Upon depleting all the health of Legion's core, its tentacles disappear as it violently bursts into flames and spins slowly to the ground, before abruptly falling through the floor and exploding into a pillar of fire.

Levarsee Darkassos can also appear if the player has  Moonlight Bolero (VI) equipped. A Levarsee Darkasso spawned from this darkana can drop a  Dark Treasure Chest once it is defeated.

← Eh'Lleve-Teh DarkassoLevatee DarkassoLevarsee Darkasso →
“
Hell operates on a trickle-down treasurenomics system, and these tiny troublemakers are sick of demonic inequality. The Levatee Darkasso collective have seen right through the unfair structure that constrains them, benefitting the treasure chest hoarding "Bosses" while leaving nothing but a few scant gems for regular enemies - but no longer! These goblinoid thieves are redistributing stolen treasures amongst the demonic masses, so please don't disturb them.
— Ars Gouda, p. 145
Levatee Darkasso is an enemy that appears in Room 1665.

## Trivia
- The Levatees seem to be inspired by the blue and green thieves from the Golden Axe series, who would usually steal some resources from the player in bonus stages but would drop more than they stole if the player successfully hit them enough.
- The Name of the Levatees seem to be a pun based on the phrase "Levati dal cazzo", which in Italian means "Get the f- out of the way" (lit. "Get off the d*ck").

← Dust ElementalLionheadMilk Elemental →
“
Some say these many-limbed beasts descended from a great Lord of Hell, whose domain is the healing arts and philosophy. A noble lineage claimed for what was clearly the result of some drunk necromancers and a sewing kit.
— Ars Gouda, p. 14
Lionhead is an enemy that appears in Inlaid Library, Boss Rash, and Mazerella. In Inlaid Library, it appears both as a normal enemy and as a boss. In Boss Rash and Mazerella, it only appears as a boss.
Killing 3,000 Lionheads is the requirement for unlocking  Yatta Cavallo. This count does not include the unlisted variants of Lionhead.
Lionhead's health is based on the player's level. Its boss variant is also resistant to knockback.

“
Makes up for the lack of a secondary head by being really, extra, super-duper demonic! They’re not overcompensating, they’re perfectly compensating.
— Ars Gouda, p. 45
Lost Twin is an enemy that appears in Dairy Plant, The Bone Zone, Boss Rash, Carlo Cart, and Laborratory. In Dairy Plant, it appears as a normal enemy, a boss, and as part of map events. In The Bone Zone and Carlo Cart, it appears as a normal enemy and a boss. In Boss Rash, it only appears as a boss if Arcanas are enabled. In Laborratory, it only appears as a boss.
Lost Twin shoots bullets every second, dealing high damage, but cannot move (excluding its racing variants) and is immune to knockback. Additionally, its boss variants' health is also based on the player's level.

“
All that glitters is not gold. Sometimes it’s bats. While such a rare creature might seem to herald the coming of something truly momentous, it might surprise you to learn that, despite appearances, they are bats, and nothing more.
— Ars Gouda, p. 130
LV128 Golden Bat is a special enemy that appears in Green Acres and Bat Country. After entering Green Acres with Hyper Mode and Hurry Mode enabled and travelling far southwest, where the background starts to appear glitched, a large amount of LV128 Golden Bats will spawn instead of the regular enemies. Defeating 128 of these bats, will unlock missingN▯ as a playable character. In Bat Country, it appears both as a normal enemy and a boss.
LV128 Golden Bat takes reduced knockback.

An alien cyborg supposedly modelled after the visually intimidating but tactically ineffectual Alien Centipede, the M78 has advanced "artificial insidiousness" to make it a true battlefield predator. Relunctantly lauded by Red Falcon infantry for its prowess on the battlefield when providing fire support, the M78 is despised for the really annoying sound it makes when it walks.

Imagine this thing crawling in through your window at night, wheezing with a whispered rasp as it shudders its way up the stairs. It approaches your bedroom, clutching the bell around its neck with a trembling hand, staring through a crack in the door. Watching. Waiting. Admiring you while you sleep. After an hour, maybe two, it cannot resist any longer and it wriggles into the room without moving the door an inch. It’s here. It’s here to lick your eyebrows. It wants the nutrients, you see, the minerals your eyebrows hide. It’s going to do this every Wednesday for a year.
— Ars Gouda, p. Fs 40

A monstrosity of sorrowful despair. A fool who fancied himself Dracula's successor created Menace in a failed bid to claim ultimate power. Upon defeating Menace for the first time, it will drop the  Serpent Gate relic.

← LionheadMilk ElementalDragon Shrimp →
“
Behold, the blasphemy of lactomancy! These homogenized horrors were formulated to pulverize and pasteurize in the name of their intolerant Milk Mages. Any who’ve tried to cream them end up torn in half… and half! This entry has been condensed for those who prefer to skim.
— Ars Gouda, p. 15
Milk Elemental is an enemy that appears in Dairy Plant, Boss Rash, Eudaimonia Machine, and Mazerella. In Dairy Plant, it first appears as a part of Swarm events, then later as a normal enemy. In Boss Rash and Mazerella, it only appears as a normal enemy. In Eudaimonia Machine, it is summoned by The Directer.
Killing a total of 3,000 Milk Elementals is the requirement for unlocking  Bianca Ramba.

← GoshadokuroOrochimarioUniconiglio →
“
Ancient legendary dragon of immense power and wisdom. With eight heads, eight tails, and a love for eightfold strong red wine, if you’re invited for a night out by it, best decline. It’ll be a nightmare to carry home.
— Ars Gouda, p. Ms 16
Orochimario is a boss that appears in Mt.Moonspell at 25:00.
Defeating Orochimario in Mt.Moonspell unlocks the stage's hyper mode.
Orochimario takes reduced knockback. It is resistant to freeze, instant kill, and debuffs.
## Behavior
Orochimario spawns with eight heads, the first head has double the health to be 1,000★ and each head adds its own health to Orochimario, which means its total health is actually 5,000★. When they take damage, Orochimario itself also take that much, they can be individually defeated. The heads can lunge at the player to attack them every 5 to 10 seconds.

## Trivia
- Persephone's bestiary entry has several references to the 2022 role-playing game Elden Ring, namely the Tarnished, the player character, and the Finger Maidens, whose goals are to assist the Tarnished in their quest to become Elden Lord. Initially, the Tarnished begins without a Finger Maiden, thus being dubbed "maidenless" by White Mask Varré.

# Pipeestrello
Bat
"Bat" redirects here. For the playable character, see Bats Bats Bats.

|

| HP | 1 - 15

| Speed | 100 - 140
| Resistances | -
| Skills | -
| Found in | Mad Forest, Gallo Tower
| {"update":"There exist tales that don't exist.","stages":"Mad Forest, Gallo Tower","resistances":"-","image":"Sprite-Pipeestrello.png","health":"1 - 15","movespeed":"100 - 140","release":"27 October 2021","skills":"-","name":"Pipeestrello","damage":"4 - 6"}
PipeestrelloBloodbath →
“
The cultural association between bat and vampire stretches back as far as folklore itself. Some claim to have seen the deathly cold visage of a Vampire Lord striding within the swirling maelstrom of these nocturnal nasties, but those people just wanted attention. They’re bats, and nothing more.
— Ars Gouda, p. 1
Pipeestrello (compare pipistrello, Italian for bat) is an enemy primarily encountered in Mad Forest and Gallo Tower, with additional appearances in Inlaid Library, Dairy Plant, Boss Rash, Whiteout, Carlo Cart, Bat Country, Tiny Bridge, Ante Chamber, and Eudaimonia Machine.
In Mad Forest, Boss Rash, Bat Country, it appears both as a normal enemy and during the Bat Swarm event. In Gallo Tower and Tiny Bridge, it only spawns as a normal enemy, while in Inlaid Library, it appears exclusively during the Bat Swarm. In Eudaimonia Machine, it is summoned by the The Directer as a special enemy during the initial phase of the stage.
There are two boss variants of Pipeestrello: Pipeestrello 4 and Pipeestrello 5
- Pipeestrello 4, also referred to as "Glowing Bat", appears in Mad Forest, Inlaid Library, Bat Country, Gallo Tower, Dairy Plant, and Carlo Cart. In Mad Forest and Inlaid Library, spawns both as a regular boss and an Arcana-carrier boss when Arcanas are enabled. In Bat Country, only the regular boss variant appears. In Gallo Tower, Dairy Plant, and Carlo Cart, only the Arcana-carrier boss can appear.
- Pipeestrello 5, also referred to as "Silver Bat", appears only in Mad Forest, Inlaid Library, and Whiteout. It spawns as a regular boss in Mad Forest and Inlaid Library, and also as an Arcana-carrier boss in Whiteout when Arcanas are enabled.
Both boss variants reappear together as regular enemies during later waves in Mad Forest.
The boss variants' health scales with the player's level. The Swarm variant is resistant to defang and moves in a fixed direction.

“
Some portraits contain the souls sold by certain witches to obtain eternal youth and health. But let's talk about the Poetrait instead.
As Poe becomes ever more aged and garlic scented, this elegant portrait becomes ever younger and smalls increasingly of lavender. Who wouldn't clamour for A Picture of Poe to Display?
— Ars Gouda, p. 136
Poetrait is an enemy that appears in Astral Stair.

## Trivia
- The name of this enemy is a pun of  Poe Ratcho, even though only one variant, out of 3, is based on Poe.
- The enemy description is a reference to The Picture of Dorian Gray, in which the titular character sells his soul so a portrait of himself ages in his place. This causes Dorian to remain youthful, while his portrait ages and visually records every one of his sins.

# Poltergeist
| Poltergeist
| 
| Bestiary ?Enemy stats as displayed in the Bestiary.See individual variants for more detailed information.
| Release | ? (edit)

| Bestiary # | 18
| HP | 5 – 10★

| Speed | 0 – 150
| Resistances | ? (edit)
| Skills | Self-destruct, HP x Level
| Found in | Gallo Tower, Cappella Magna
| {"image":"Sprite-Poltergeist.png","stages":"Gallo Tower, Cappella Magna","movespeed":"0 – 150","skills":"Self-destruct, HP x Level","health":"5 – 10","damage":"3"}
← Sig.ra RossiPoltergeistHag →
“
There’s a perfectly rational explanation for this. Perhaps these items are the product of a mage’s trickery, or automata dreamed up by Gallo’s gibbering scientists. Maybe each one is filled with a colony of vindictive ants that push them around from inside. Let’s eliminate those possibilities before we mention ghosts.
— Ars Gouda, p. 18
Poltergeist is an enemy that appears in Gallo Tower and Cappella Magna. In Gallo Tower the candelabra variants appear in the map event, Poltergeist Roulette, which can be triggered by stepping on a trap. In Cappella Magna, the gem-like variants are spawned as a part of The Trickster's attack pattern. 
Poltergeist's health is based on the player's level. If the player gets close enough, it triggers its self-destruct, exploding 2 seconds later for 50 damage. It has no collision and does not take knockback.

Amphibious conquerors who wish to be feared but cannot conquer the universal truth that frogs are funny. Rana Combatants have proved powerful largely as their enemies are often too busy laughing to fight back. Their Amphibian Assault techniques have conquered foes and left their summoners consumed by fits of laughter in Grelon and Mare Nostrum, leaving the froggy fighters with a frquent confused malaise. If they only wished to make the world a happier place, the Rana would want for nothing.

This article is about enemy. For character, see Sargon.
DLC Content (Legacy of the Bloodmoon)
This article primarily features content only available in DLC, which may not be available in all copies of the game.
| Sargon [DLC]

| Bestiary ?Enemy stats as displayed in the Bestiary.See individual variants for more detailed information.
| Release | 28 August 2026 (Update)

| HP | 60,000
| Power | 25

| Resistances | Freeze, Rosary, Debuff, Knockback
| Skills | -
| Found in | Red Moon Manor
| {"stages":"Red Moon Manor","update":"Legacy of the Bloodmoon is out now!","dlc":"bm","resistances":"Freeze, Rosary, Debuff, Knockback","image":"","health":"60,000","movespeed":"180","release":"28 August 2026","skills":"-","name":"Sargon","damage":"25"}
← AshtartSargonBaal'Thasar →
“
A scholar king of a long-lost empire, Sargon's ambition could never be satiated.
He could look out from the highest tower of his palace and know that every land his gaze touched belonged to him, yet still it was not enough. During the first Blood Moon, a new and twisted idea crept into his mind: if he could see the stars, then he should also own them.
His obsession with conquering the impossible dragged him further and further away from his duties as king, and so his people began to mock him as the "king of the universe".
He turned to the teachings of Baal'Thasar of the Red Moon, for eternal life was surely his right. At night, he would stare into the darkest spots in the sky. He claimed the universe whispered to him in those moments, revealing secrets and knowledge no human could ever attain alone.
With every secret learned, Sargon lost another piece of his humanity. At last, he found himself trapped in a hellish dimension: an infinite universe of his own. A universe devoid of any other life. A universe so miserable that all of its stars blaze at their brightest, consuming themselves in haste, unable to bear the anguish of bringing light to a forever lifeless void.
Now Sargon desperately tries to escape his prison. He blinks back into our world without control, only for the fabric of his universe to pull him away again. Cold stars shimmer through his fleeting form: a warning to look away.
Stare too long into Sargon's void, and you too might find yourself seduced and trapped in an infinite universe of your own.
— Ars Gouda, p. Bm 19
Sargon is an enemy that appears in Red Moon Manor. He locates on the north on the Manor, appears after stepping on the button, and is always marked with a question mark (?) on the map.

Bones made of blood. Or blood made of bones. There’s blood involved somewhere. These rufescent ruffians are out to spill even more of it.
