Scarleton is an enemy that appears in Gallo Tower and The Bone Zone. In Gallo Tower it first appears as a boss and later also as a normal enemy and in The Bone Zone only as a normal enemy. It cannot drop a Treasure Chest.
Scarleton has 3 lives, meaning that it can resurrect twice. However, if killed by Pentagram, Gorgeous Moon, Rosary or Heaven Sword, it will not resurrect. Each time it dies it drops an Experience Gem.

Unlike most enemies, Scorej-Oni does not spawn as a part a wave or map event, but can be found naturally far east, around 20 tilesets (map tiles) distance, of the starting area in Tiny Bridge.

← Dragon ShrimpSig.ra RossiPoltergeist →
“
There are works so incomprehensible in theme and meaning as to fracture the reader’s very grasp of reality. Torn from the material world through sheer force of their disbelief in it, these scholarly fools are only one last thought away from volatile oblivion.
— Ars Gouda, p. 17
Sig.ra Rossi, or Red Shade, is an enemy that appears in Inlaid Library. It only appears as a part of the map event, Shade Bomb. 
Sig.ra Rossi's health is based on the player's level.
## Behavior
It chases the player and upon getting close enough it triggers its self-destruct, exploding 2 seconds later for 50 damage. It does not collide with other enemies.

← ECMASlimeSinistronzTwin Skulls →
“
Burner of libraries, champion of crashes, hogger of memories, this puzzle box is made to look smart and appealing while it's actually full of half-digested piles of sludge that slowly seep through its surface, making anyone who fell for its enticing presentation and dared to touch it end up sticky, icky, slow, frustrated, bombarded by soliciting attempts, and suddenly be charged hidden fees
Also a necessary evil.
— Ars Gouda, p. 87
Sinistronz is an enemy that appears in Space 54.

## Trivia
- Sinistronz is designed to resemble the logo for the Unity Game Engine, the current game engine that all versions of Vampire Survivors runs on. The enemy description in the Beastiary references common issues developers have with Unity, both as a program and as a business. During a Reddit AMA, when asked if Unity would be his game engine of choice for any future projects; Luca 'Poncle' Galante, creator of Vampire Survivors simply answered "lol no thank you!".

Even as infant demons, these gore-craving boors would graffiti the words "BLOOD" on the walls of the underworld and carve it into their satanic schooldesks. Among the most irritating of all hellspawn, they constantly chant about their abounding bloodlust, loudly proclaiming that they just cannot get enough of that vital red stuff. Frequently on the forefront of any infernal invasion force wherever reality intermingles with their evil nether-realm, these demons are always sent to the frontlines, mostly because everyone just wants them to go away.

| {"update":"There exist tales that don't exist.","stages":"Mad Forest, Dairy Plant, Gallo Tower, others","resistances":"-","image":"Sprite-Skeleton.png","health":"15","movespeed":"100","release":"27 October 2021","skills":"-","name":"Skeleton","damage":"10"}
← ScarletonSkeletonZombie →
“
What army of darkness would be complete without skeletons? They are highly disciplined, incredibly vengeful, and very unionized!
— Ars Gouda, p. 6
Skeleton is an enemy primarily encountered in Mad Forest, Dairy Plant, Gallo Tower, and The Bone Zone, with additional appearances in Boss Rash and Eudaimonia Machine.
In Mad Forest, Gallo Tower, The Bone Zone, and Boss Rash, it appears exclusively as a normal enemy. In Dairy Plant, it spawns both as a normal enemy and during Swarm map events. In Eudaimonia Machine, it is summoned by The Directer as a special enemy during the second phase of the stage.
Defeating a total of 3,000 Skeletons unlocks the character  Mortaccio.

# Sketamari
| Sketamari
| 
| Bestiary ?Enemy stats as displayed in the Bestiary.See individual variants for more detailed information.
| Release | 4 August 2022 (Update)
| Theme | No
| Bestiary # | 93
| HP | 10,800
| Power | 40
| Speed | 220
| Resistances | Freeze, Rosary, Instant kill, Debuff
| Skills | Fixed Direction
| Found in | The Bone Zone
| {"id":"SKETAMARI","update":"Patch 0.10.0","health":"10,800","image":"Sprite-SKETAMARI.png","stages":"The Bone Zone","resistances":"Freeze, Rosary, Instant kill, Debuff","release":"4 August 2022","skills":"Fixed Direction","movespeed":"220","damage":"40"}
← SkeletoneSketamariSun Atlantean →
“
Said to be the result of attempts to create a “Bone Moon” for reasons that defy all sense and logic. Driven by hundreds of minds with a singular purpose, it is drawn to places of high skeletal activity with the intent of adding to its chittering mass and growing ever bigger. How would a Bone Moon even work? Pray this horrifying absurdity never comes to pass.
— Ars Gouda, p. 93
Sketamari is a special enemy that appears in The Bone Zone after obtaining the Yellow Sign. Unlike most enemies, it does not spawn as part a wave or map event, but instead can be found 5 tileset due south of The Bone Zone's center, traveling back and forth horizontally with a range of 5 tilesets in each direction. Defeating Sketamari for the first time drops  Forbidden Scrolls of Morbane.
## Behaviour
Sketamari is made up of many skeletal enemies, including Skeletons, Skullinos, Skeleton Panthers, Skeletones, Winged Skeletons, and Skullones. It rolls back and forth to the south of the centre of the stage, and is marked with a question mark (?) on the map. As the player moves closer to it, the sound of it rolling will become louder.
Sketamari is immune to freeze, instant kill effects such as  Pentagram and  Rosary, and other debuffs.
## Absorption
Enemies that touch Sketamari will be instantly defeated and absorbed (including Reapers), yielding no Experience, Chests, or other drops. 
Each time Sketamari absorbs an enemy, it will grow larger.
- Starting at 0.25, its scale grows by 0.001 until 1.
- At 1, its scale grows by 0.00085 per enemy until 1.5.
- At 1.5, its scale grows by 0.00065 per enemy until 2.
- At 2, its scale grows by 0.0005 per enemy until 3.
- At 3, its scale grows by 0.00025 per enemy until 4, after which it will not grow anymore.
The amount of health gained from an absorbed enemy is calculated based on the scale via the following procedure:
- The formula Math.max(1, scale) is used to get a health divider X.
- Then X is used in Math.min(2,500, 0.2 * enemy health / X), where the enemy health is multiplied by 0.2 and then divided by Math.max(1, scale) "X", the lower value between the division and 2,500 is taken.
- Due to the previous step, the divisor will not be less than 1, increasing the value, or greater than 4, as the scale will not go above it.
- Even when a Reaper gets absorbed, it will only give it 2,500 health at max. Other sources also slowly give less as it gets larger because of the health divider is based on its scale.
- The final value is then added to both its current HP and max HP.
If the player is further than 60,000 units away, this will not happen. Although, most enemies despawn already at a much shorter distance, preventing them from being absorbed.
## Radius and speed
Sketamari's radius is 200 times its scale. The bigger the radius is, the slower it also rolls. It has various speed multipliers:
- Its general movement speed is determined by the formula Math.max(0.5 x player's speed, player's speed - 0.01 x radius)
- When it takes knockback, it moves backwards at 90% of its normal speed. Once it reaches a radius of 150 or higher, it will not be pushed back from knockback anymore.
## Counter strategy
For tips, character, and build options that can help in defeating this enemy, see: Guide:Defeating the Sketamari.
## Variants

| Found in: The Bone Zone
| Skills: Fixed Direction
| Health10,800 | Power40 | MSpeed220 | KB (max)1 (1) | Res-Fr1.1 | Res-KYes | Res-DBYes | Res-DF? (edit) | Weak-Fi? (edit) | XP100
## Appearances

## Trivia
- Sketamari's name is most likely a reference to katamaris from the Katamari series. These objects, much like Sketamari, roll around and pick up objects, gradually becoming larger.
- If the player keeps looking at Sketamari's entry in the Bestiary for long enough, it will start growing, eventually getting big enough to cover the entire screen.

# Sneaky Head
SneakyHead
| Sneaky Head
| 
| Bestiary ?Enemy stats as displayed in the Bestiary.See individual variants for more detailed information.
| Release | 2 January 2022 (Update)
| Theme | No
| Bestiary # | 22
| HP | 1 – 25
| Power | 1
| Speed | 240
| Resistances | -
| Skills | Floaty, Fixed Direction
| Found in | Inlaid Library
| {"update":"Patch 0.2.5: new stage and achievements","resistances":"-","image":"Sprite-Sneaky Head.png","stages":"Inlaid Library","movespeed":"240","release":"2 January 2022","skills":"Floaty, Fixed Direction","health":"1 – 25","damage":"1"}
← MummySneaky HeadHarzia →
“
Ranked among the most detested creatures in the land, they find crude sport in pushing unsuspecting victims from ledges when they’re most vulnerable. Faced with an enemy that neither jumps nor climbs, however, they find themselves rather powerless.
— Ars Gouda, p. 22
Sneaky Head is an enemy that appears in Inlaid Library, Moongolow, Boss Rash, Tiny Bridge, and Mazerella. In Inlaid Library, it appears as a normal enemy, as a boss and as a part of Medusa Swarm and Medusa Wall map events. In Moongolow, Tiny Bridge, and Mazerella, it only appears as a normal enemy. In Boss Rash, it only appears as a boss.
Sneaky Head's moves horizontally across the screen in a wavy pattern, instead of homing to the player. The unit has a "fast" variant, which has higher movespeed. The "aggro" and boss variants have normal movespeed, and do not have the fixed movement restriction, chasing the player like most other enemies. Its boss variant's health is based on the player's level, and it takes reduced knockback.

- The enemy's name and design reference the Among Us Eyes meme art trend originated in 2022, wherein online artists, at first by accident but later purposefully, rendered their characters' pupils in the simplified shape of an Among Us crewmate.
- The Ars Gouda description for the enemy references also Castlevania's Granfaloon, also known as Legion.

← DoppelgangerThe CreatureBugbear →
“
He wants to be your sledgehammer. While this tome uses his most popularized name for the sake of readability, those more learned know it to be a misnomer. All scholars know that Dr. The Creature was the name of the creator, not the monster.

| {"update":"Patch 1.0","health":"-1","movespeed":"0","image":"Sprite-The Directer (Bestiary).png","stages":"Eudaimonia Machine","resistances":"Freeze, Rosary, Debuff, Instant kill, Defang","release":"20 October 2022","skills":"-","theme":"Cosmic Delight","damage":"0"}
← LV128 Golden BatThe DirecterAstral Elemental →
“
Boundless gifts. Sights beyond reality. Glimpses of the unthinkable.
— Ars Gouda, p. 131
The Directer is a special boss enemy that appears in Eudaimonia Machine, and is the final boss of Vampire Survivors.
The Directer cannot be damaged. It takes no knockback and is resistant to freeze, instant kill effects, and debuffs.
## Behavior
Entering Eudaimonia Machine after collecting the  Seventh Trumpet and  Gracia's Mirror and conversing with The Directer for a third time will cause it to begin a battle with the player. The Directer appears towards the left of the player with seven masks, or fragments, resembling the Atlantean's heads (referred to in the final battle of the Ode to Castlevania DLC as the Aspects of Seawinds, Sun, Volcano, Moon, City, Stone, and Black respectively) circling around it. Similarly to the  White Hand, The Directer moves with the player's view, meaning it is impossible to get closer or further away from it. The player cannot damage The Directer or the Atlantean heads for now. The Directer summons a group of 50  Swarm Pipeestrellos or 16  Yellow Reaper Trainees, selected randomly, to attack the player every 5 seconds.
## Phase 1
After 30 seconds, The Directer also starts to throw damaging Golden Egg Bullets at the player, and the enemy pool is expanded to include groups of 24  Dust Elementals, 24  Milk Elementals, and 24  Skullinos. Each summon cycle increases the amount of summoned enemies by 1. After another 30 seconds have passed and the player is at least level 7, the battle will enter phase 2.
## Phase 2
The playable area opens up, and the background starts featuring the previous five stages behind the black frame. The enemy swarms also change based on the background.
- Mad Forest: 50  Swarm Pipeestrellos + 24  Zombies
- Inlaid Library: 12  Mummies + 12  Ghosts
- Dairy Plant: 12  Lizard Pawns + 24  Mermen
- Gallo Tower: 24  Skulorossos + 12  Testa di Manos
- Cappella Magna: 16  Yellow Reaper Trainees + 24  Skeletons
Each summon cycle in this phase increases the amount of summoned enemies by 1. The Directer's hands also move to the right side of the screen and now throw two Golden Egg Bullets at once.
After 60 seconds have passed and the player is at least level 14, the Atlantean heads lose their invulnerability and break upon receiving a hit. After breaking all seven heads, the battle enters phase 3.
## Phase 3
The background becomes engulfed in flames and The Directer summons 5 skulls and 2 eyeballs circling around it. Like the Atlantean heads, these are also invulnerable at the moment. The Directer will now summon groups of 12 Unknowns, increased by 1 every attack cycle, and attack the player with damaging zones of explosions, similarly to  The Ender, or by shooting exploding eyes at them, indicated by circular yellow zones, similarly to  The Maddener in Holy Forbidden, every 5 seconds. The possible attacks are 50 shots at 0.06 second intervals, 10 shots at 0.03 second intervals, 20 shots at 0.1 second intervals, two damaging zones close to center and two damaging zones close to sides. If the player has any Revivals left, one of The Directer's hands will also drag in the  White Hand from the right side of the screen to kill the player when it attacks.
Once the player is at least level 19 and has no Revivals left and it has been at least 60 seconds since the phase started, the skulls and eyes become breakable and the player can proceed to the next phase after breaking all seven of them.
## Phase 4
The background changes to the first phase's background but more illuminated, and The Directer creates clusters of coins and gems circling around it. Damaging a cluster will grant the player 0.5 Gold or 3 experience respectively. Swarms of  Weak Reapers are also spawned. Even if the player dies in this phase, they will be automatically revived by The Directer's hands.
Once the player has reached level 22 and at least 45 seconds have passed, the clusters become breakable and breaking them allows the player to proceed to the final phase.
## Phase 5
The background breaks into space and The Directer disappears, before its hands appear at the top and bottom of the screen and start showering the player with  Gold Coins and  Experience Gems. The player can move around, but they will not find anything except for a beating heart that looks similar to the  Pummarola. After a while, the player will be transferred to the credits. The experience gems are not real and will not allow the player to unlock experience level achievements, but the gold coins are. This can be a reliable short-term gold farm as the battle only takes 4 to 5 minutes.
Defeating The Directer and viewing the ending credits for the first time unlocks  Greatest Jubilee.

| {"update":"Patch 0.8.0","health":"1270 – 2550","movespeed":"150","image":"Sprite-The Ender.png","stages":"Cappella Magna, Boss Rash","resistances":"Freeze, Debuff, Instant kill, Knockback","release":"7 July 2022","skills":"HP x Level","theme":"Cosmic Delight","damage":"50"}
← The BlinderThe EnderMimic Season One →
“
When the power of friendship goes planet. The ultimate personification of oblivion, The Ender encapsulates all aspects of true despair - Death, Madness, Obsession, Deceit, and Being Wet. Its arrival signifies true futility, a final warning to those who’ve embarked on a doomed quest and refuse to understand that what they seek is not here, in this world or the next.
— Ars Gouda, p. 125
The Ender is a special boss enemy that appears in Cappella Magna and Boss Rash. It appears to be an amalgamation of  The Reaper,  The Trickster,  The Stalker,  The Drowner, and  The Maddener merged into one.
It acts as the final enemy in Cappella Magna, spawning shortly after the player reaches the 30:00 mark until it is defeated. Defeating it unlocks  Game Killer (0), and also drops the  Great Gospel on the floor to be picked up. In Boss Rash, it spawns as a wave boss. The Yellow Sign must be collected before The Ender will spawn.
The Ender's health is based on the player's level and it is resistant to knockback, freeze, instant kill and debuffs. It also has a shield that temporarily absorbs damage.
## Behavior
If the player has obtained the Yellow Sign upon starting a run in Cappella Magna, surviving until the 30:00 mark causes all remaining enemies to disappear and the stage's background to fade to black. This is followed by a 30 second countdown, before depicting a brief cutscene of transparent silhouettes of The Reaper, The Trickster, The Stalker, The Drowner, and The Maddener flying towards the top of the screen and merging into a single entity. When The Ender finally spawns, the background changes into a swirling purple and white void.
The Ender moves towards the player and fires Scythe Bullets at them once every 5 seconds. It also creates beams of  Reaper Trainees, coffins, fire explosions, and sprite work of the base weapons. The zones periodically deal 12 damage in the area and last 10 seconds, with the exception of the Reaper Trainee zones that last 5 seconds. During the first 90 seconds of the fight, The Ender also has a shield that absorbs all incoming damage. After the shield expires after 90 seconds, all of the damage absorbed will be instantly dealt to The Ender.
The attack rate of the scythes and the pattern the beams changes as more damage is dealt to The Ender. The interval between scythes decreases linearly based on the percentage of health remaining, to a minimum of 0.5 seconds. The beam pattern changes based on the formula 9−rnd(9hpmaxHP)+1:
- Trainees (horizontal)
- Coffins (vertical)
- Double trainees (h) + double coffins (v)
- Explosions (h)
- Double explosions (h)
- Weapons (v)
- Double weapons (v)
- Trainees (h) + coffins (v) + weapons (v) + explosions (h)
When The Ender is killed, it drops the  Great Gospel, and once it has been obtained, The Ender will never reappear in Capella Magna again. After collecting the Great Gospel, the  White Hand appears and ends the run.
## Boss Rash
In Boss Rash, The Ender's health and shield duration are reduced to half, and the duration of its beam attacks is 40% shorter. Defeating it also grants experience, unlike in Cappella Magna. Additionally, the shield it gains at the start of the fight now only lasts 45 seconds before it expires.
## Adventures
## The World of Dark and Light
The Ender is also the final boss in the second Adventure "The World of Light and Dark", and appears to have the same characteristics as its original encounter in Cappella Magna.

| {"update":"Patch 0.6.1","stages":"Holy Forbidden, Cappella Magna, Boss Rash, Carlo Cart","image":"Sprite-The Maddener.png","health":"2,550","resistances":"Freeze, Debuff, Knockback","release":"23 May 2022","skills":"HP x Level","movespeed":"300","damage":"50"}
← The DrownerThe MaddenerThe Blinder →
“
A Reaper whose true form lies beyond the edge of sanity. Bearing the power to alter reality itself, it exists to confuse and disorient, and feeds on the mental anguish inspired in its victims. When robbed of its ability to beguile, however, it falls prey to the very fear and bewilderment it causes in others, its very sense of self overridden by sheer panic.
— Ars Gouda, p. 123
The Maddener is an enemy that makes its first appearance in Holy Forbidden as a special enemy, and in Cappella Magna, Boss Rash, and Carlo Cart as an event or boss enemy.
Maddener's health is based on the player's level. It is immune to knockback, freeze, instant kill and debuffs.
## Instances
In Holy Forbidden, it appears a special enemy with a unique behavior in contrast to its other instances.
In Cappella Magna, it is guaranteed to spawn at 0:10 as a special event if the player has not yet defeated The Ender. Since it is spawned by a special event instead of being a part of the wave, it cannot appear in Green Acres. After defeating The Ender, it will no longer spawn in this stage.
In Boss Rash, it spawns as a wave boss.
In Carlo Cart, it is summoned by the Timed Enemy Spawn map event and spawns as a boss at a later wave, appearing twice in the stage.
## Behavior
## Holy Forbidden
See Holy Forbidden#Waves
Maddener appears in its alternative form, seemingly summoning other enemies and attacking the player in a special sequence. At the end, Maddener will directly attack the player, instantly killing them if they come in contact. If the player collects the Rosary at the end of the stage, the Maddener's true form is revealed, and it runs away.
## Cappella Magna and Boss Rash
A weakened version of Maddener spawns in Cappella Magna and Boss Rash, with less health and movement speed. Other than its ability to change the appearance of some enemies while it is alive in Cappella Magna, it behaves similarly to the Stalker, chasing the player around and dealing massive damage on contact.
## Carlo Cart
The Maddener behaves similarly to its counterparts from Capella Magna and Boss Rash; attacking by directly approaching the player. Other than its attack pattern, it does not change enemies' appearances and has a health-scaling equivalent to its Holy Forbidden counterpart.

## Trivia
- Given it's yellow attire and it's power over the concept of "Madness", The Maddener is most likely based off of the King In Yellow, a mysterious and malevolent supernatural and gothic entity from the book The King in Yellow by Robert W. Chambers.
- The King within the story would inflict madness upon whomever looked at it, similar to how The Maddener would change the player's perspective upon appearing.

| {"update":"There exist tales that don't exist.","name":"The Reaper","movespeed":"1,200","image":"Sprite-The_Reaper.png","stages":"All normal, challenge and bonus stages","resistances":"Freeze, Instant kill, Debuff","release":"27 October 2021","skills":"HP x Level","health":"655,350","damage":"65,535"}
← Stage KillerThe ReaperThe Trickster →
“
Run from him. Defy him. Maybe even kill him, should you prove so arrogant. No matter the outcome, Lord Death always wins in the end. You can close your eyes, turn your head, and scream into the void, but with time you all shall wear his mask.
— Ars Gouda, p. 119
The Reaper is an enemy that spawns once every minute after the time limit of a stage has been reached, unless endless mode has been turned on. In Eudaimonia Machine, a more vulnerable variant of the Reaper is summoned by  The Directer. 
## Behavior
Once spawned, The Reaper will quickly rush to the player's character and deal 65,535 damage to them, ending the run. Player is rewarded 500 for surviving to the end. The player can revive if any Revivals are left, increasing the bonus every time by stacking +100 Gold (e.g. total bonus for 3 revives is 500 + 100 + 200 + 300 = 1,100 Gold). This bonus lasts up to +400 Gold, after which it will reset back to +100 Gold for any remaining Revivals. 
The Reaper's health pool is set at 655,350, further multiplied by the player's level when it spawns. The Reaper has -50% knockback resistance, causing it to get dragged closer to the player whenever it takes a hit, instead of being pushed away. It is possible to freeze The Reaper using weapons or items that creates freezing effects such as  Clock Lancet,  Orologion, or a weapon affected by  Jail of Crystal (XIV).
Killing The Reaper the first time unlocks the secret character  Mask of the Red Death as a playable character. If The Reaper was defeated with either  Infinite Corridor or  Crimson Shroud, it will drop 5  Golden Eggs and trigger the  White Hand to appear and end the run definitely.

## Strategies
## Crimson Shroud + Infinite Corridor
As of version 0.6.1, the most accessible method of defeating The Reaper involves having the Crimson Shroud and the Infinite Corridor: evolutions of Laurel and Clock Lancet respectively. Doing so has the following requirements:
- Unlocking Hyper mode on 4 normal stages unlocks Moongolow.
- If the Yellow Sign has not been obtained, a special boss will spawn near the end of a Moongolow run when using a non-secret character. Defeating it grants access to Holy Forbidden once the 15-minute time limit is reached.
- Within Holy Forbidden, collect the Yellow Sign.
- The following items will now always spawn on every stage: Metaglio Left, Metaglio Right, Silver Ring and Gold Ring.
- Having a level 7 Clock Lancet, level 9 Silver Ring and level 9 Gold Ring will allow evolving the Clock Lancet into Infinite Corridor.
- Having a level 7 Laurel, level 9 Metaglio Left and level 9 Metaglio Right will allow evolving the Laurel into Crimson Shroud.
- Open 2 Treasure Chests and you will receive Crimson Shroud and Infinite Corridor.
Crimson Shroud caps damage to 10 at a time, making The Reaper's attack far less effective if it attacks you. Crimson Shroud also does amplified retaliatory damage, enough to kill The Reaper without Corridor, though this may take some time. However Infinite Corridor halves all enemies health, making it possible to defeat The Reaper in a fairly short amount of time.
The above strategy can still be done with an unevolved Laurel, though it would be considerably riskier. If skipping Crimson Shroud, be sure to have mastered your usage of Clock Lancet/Infinite Corridor's freeze effect, in order to avoid being attacked by The Reaper as much as possible. Also, be aware that every minute, a new Reaper spawns, which would then need to be frozen near immediately while also likely taking a Laurel charge.
Defeating the Reaper with an Infinite Corridor and without a Laurel at all is not impossible. While normally very risky due to the high probability of dying before being able to freeze the Reaper again, Mirage Robe or its evolution J'Odore can also lock the Reaper in place and, unlike Infinite Corridor itself, help protect the player from all sides. This represents a way for freeze-focused builds to defeat the Reaper without having to waste an item slot on a Laurel. Enough Revives obtained through Tirajisú and Awake (IV) may also allow the player to simply "weather" the Reaper's attacks.
This strategy is required for The Reaper to drop Golden Eggs, and is also the only way for the Reaper's death to summon the White Hand.
## Level 200 Toastie
Prior to version 0.6.1, defeating The Reaper was intended to be done with the character Toastie. The following special game mechanics apply to this character:
- Toastie begins with 1 HP. At level 100, it gains an additional 9,998 HP.
- At level 200, Toastie gains an additional 65,520 Armor. This reduces the damage from The Reaper's attack to 15.
- By using the Armor PowerUp and Armor passive item, an additional 8 Armor can be earned, reducing the damage from The Reaper's attack to 7.
- By using the Awake Arcana in combination with the Revival PowerUp and Tirajisú, an additional 6 Armor can be earned by reviving six times. This further reduces the damage from The Reaper's attack to 1.
A level 200 Toastie can go toe-to-toe in combat with The Reaper. Max Armor used in conjunction with direct healing from Bloody Tear or Soul Eater and/or passive healing from the Recovery PowerUp and Pummarola will allow Toastie to withstand the Reapers' onslaught indefinitely. Over the course of several minutes, Toastie will deal enough damage to defeat The Reaper.
The challenge with this approach is not in the mechanics of the battle with The Reaper, but rather in devising and executing a strategy to get Toastie to level 200 with the desired stats, all within the time limit.
## Sanctuary
Main article: Sanctuary
The Sanctuary weapon occasionally erases all enemies on screen, including enemies immune to instant kills. Paired with a Clock Lancet or Orologion to freeze The Reaper, or a Familiar to distract it, Sanctuary can erase all Reapers present in the screen. This effect may or may not be intended thus it may get patched out in a future update.
## Other Strategies
Main article: /Other Strategies
Before the introduction of Infinite Corridor, other strategies were used to kill The Reaper, mostly relying on certain exploits involving the game mechanics. For more information, see here.
In summary:
- Suor Clerici's temporary Area bonus can be used in Inlaid Library to get Runetracers perpetually stuck in the geometry. By hiding behind the projectiles for protection, Clerici can survive the full 30 minutes at level 2, thereby spawning The Reaper with relatively low HP (1.2 million). Gathering the red experience gem which contains all the XP and Treasure Chests at the 30-minute mark will give enough levels and evolution options to assemble a build capable of defeating The Reaper.
- Prior to verison 0.6.1, Bone would get stuck if spawned inside a large enemy that has been frozen in place. This is because it attempts to bounce off of enemies, but being inside one means it has nowhere to go and it bounces in place very rapidly. With Clock Lancet, Bone could be used to deal very high damage to The Reaper in very little time.
- The character MissingN▯ could be used to defeat The Reaper. Its stats were all randomized every game, and if Max Health rolled a negative number, the character would be invulnerable to all damage. This made defeating The Reaper trivial, but very time-consuming.
- The character Smith IV or Avatar Infernas could be used to defeat The Reaper. Due to both characters reducing cooldowns by -0.25% per level and there is no limit, past -87.5% cooldown, Laurel's shield regenerates faster than the player can take damage. This made defeating The Reaper trivial, but very time consuming.
- The above strategy can also be executed with any other character using either the Paranormal Scan or the scaling from Hidden Anathema (III) to reduce cooldown instead. However, without extreme luck, it may be difficult to achieve a sufficient cooldown reduction before the Reaper spawns. Mirage Robe with enough Amount and Cooldown can be effective at staving off the Reapers until such reduction can be achieved, as, unlike the Clock Lancet, it protects the character from all sides simultaneously. Because Reapers do not give any EXP, the character will not gain any more levels once all EXP gems have been collected, so the Reapers' health will not increase, whereas the character will continuously raise their stats, slowly improving their ability to kill Reapers over time. Cosmo Pavone with Impostongue can be used to defeat Reapers in a relatively small amount of time thanks to the weapon's insane damage output.
- By using Game Killer (0) to keep her level 1 cooldown bonus infinitely and acquiring Sword Brothers, Porta Ladonna can become permanently invincible to everything short of the White Hand, including the Reaper's attack.
## Trivia
- The Reaper is based off of Death, one of the main antagonists of the Castlevania franchise.
- Its robe and posture have a striking resemblance to Death from Castlevania: Bloodlines.

| {"id":"BOSS_TRICKSTER_NORMAL","update":"Patch 0.7.2","health":"2,550","image":"Sprite-The Trickster.png","stages":"Inlaid Library, Cappella Magna, Carlo Cart","resistances":"Freeze, Debuff, Knockback","release":"9 June 2022","skills":"HP x Level","movespeed":"150","damage":"50"}
← The ReaperThe TricksterThe Stalker →
“
A Reaper that specializes in murder by illusion. Treasure hunters especially fall foul of the Trickster’s deadly jokes, eagerly pocketing the gems it manifests and learning all too late that they’ve stuffed their pantaloons with supernatural explosives. Loves to be the death of the party.
— Ars Gouda, p. 120
The Trickster is an enemy that appears in Cappella Magna, Inlaid Library, Boss Rash, and Carlo Cart.
Trickster's health is based on the player's level, and it is immune to knockback, freeze, and debuffs.
## Instances
In Cappella Magna, it is guaranteed to spawn at 15:10 as a special event if the player has not yet defeated The Ender. Since it is spawned by a special event instead of being a part of the wave, it cannot appear in Green Acres. After defeating The Ender, it will no longer spawn in this stage.
In Inlaid Library, it appears as a special enemy if the player enters the stage with inverse mode enabled and has defeated The Directer at least once. It is located beside a piano due far east of the player's starting location. 
In Boss Rash, it spawns as a wave boss.
In Carlo Cart, it is summoned by the Timed Enemy Spawn map event and spawns as a boss at a later wave, appearing twice in the stage.
## Behavior
The Trickster periodically summons ellipsoid rings of 48 Poltergeist Gems around the player that self-destruct when approached. The gems lasts for 30 seconds and will not despawn if the player moves away from them. Trickster can also chase and attack the player directly.
The Trickster summons two rings upon spawning and one every 30 seconds thereafter. Trickster randomly summons either a large or a small ring of Poltergeist Gems. The gems in the larger ring remain stationary, however, the gems in the smaller ring will home at the player.
The Trickster is instantly killed upon getting damaged by  Pentagram,  Gorgeous Moon,  Infinite Corridor,  Crimson Shroud,  Victory Sword,  Rosary, or  Grenade [DLC]. If Trickster is defeated with any of the aforementioned weapons and items, a red ghost will appear in the bottom-right corner of the screen, accompanied by a jingle. Trickster also takes 10 times more damage from breaking Stained Glass windows. When The Trickster dies, all remaining Poltergeist Gems explode.
## Inlaid Library
The Trickster remains stationary but can still perform attacks regardless. Once defeated, the player will be able to access the piano it was sitting by. If the correct melody is played, the player will be transported to a hidden area.
## Carlo Cart
The Trickster does not perform its usual move of conjuring Poltergeist Gems; instead, it can only attack by directly approaching the player.

← Space HunterTri-BlunderChickenfantry →
“
Insanity is doing the same thing over and over again and expecting different results. Unless you ask for the same thing 13 times, then you know what happens? Mecha-Gyorunton!
— Ars Gouda, p. 72
Tri-Blunder is an enemy that appears in Laborratory. It appears as a normal enemy, a boss, and as part of map events.
Tri-Blunder is immune to knockback, freeze, debuffs, and defang. Its boss variants' health is based on the player's level. Furthermore, the base and boss variants do not collide with walls or other enemies.

## Trivia
- Tri-Blunder's bestiary description is a reference to the Steam user BlunderingFool, who repeatedly asked for the addition of a "mecha Gyorunton" character in the game no less than 13 times since 2022.
- It's name is a blend of "Tri-Anchor" and BlunderingFool's user name, further referencing them.

← ArchdemonTrinacriaStage Killer →
“
Created to reach the stars, but she's gone much, much farther than that. She tore a hole in our universe, a gateway to another dimension. A dimension of pure chaos. Pure… evil. Look at her, isn't she beautiful?
— Ars Gouda, p. 117
Trinacria is an enemy that appears in Cappella Magna, Boss Rash, Moongolow and Holy Forbidden. In Cappella Magna, it first appears as a boss and later also as a normal enemy. In Boss Rash and Moongolow, it only appears as a boss. In Holy Forbidden, it appears as an event enemy, a large amount of them being spawned as the player approaches the end of the corridor.
Defeating Trinacria (boss) in Cappella Magna is the requirement for unlocking the stage's hyper mode.
Trinacria takes reduced knockback. Its unlisted variants' health is also based on the player's level and they are resistant to freeze, instant kill and debuffs.

## Trivia
- Trinacria's three legs form the triskelion, an ancient Greek symbol associated with Hecate, the goddess of magic and crossroads.
- Trinacria's name is the same as that of the flag of Sicily, which also features a very similar figure.
- Trinacria (boss) has the same statline as Gallotrice from Dairy Plant and Gallo Tower and Manticore from Gallo Tower.
- The description is a reference to the horror sci-fi film Event Horizon, in which Dr. Weir comments on the true evil that their invention had achieved during its journey through space. Trinacria's twisted limbs and bloody eyes are similar to the things done to members of the space voyage throughout the film.

← Undead WitchUndead Sassy WitchArchon Spada →
“
Giovanna resented being assigned Mage at birth, and proudly took up the way of the broom and pointed hat. Her sisters, who claim they want to “protect witches’ spaces,” have sworn to oppose her and all who fight by her side. Maybe one day they’ll accept that anybody can be a Sassy Witch if they truly want to be.
— Ars Gouda, p. 30
Undead Sassy Witch is an enemy that appears in Inlaid Library and Boss Rash. In Inlaid Library, it appears both as a normal enemy and as a boss. In Boss Rash, it only appears as a boss.
Undead Sassy Witch takes increased knockback. The boss variant instead takes reduced knockback, its health is based on the player's level and it is resistant to freeze.

## Trivia
- Undead Sassy Witch has the same statline as Archon Spada and Archon Disco, both of which appear in Gallo Tower.
- These three are also the only enemies in the game that take increased knockback.
- In the bestiary, “assigned Mage at birth” is a play on words, referencing “AMAB”, or “assigned male at birth”. This is especially notable, since Undead Mages are described as specifically “boys possessed of prodigious magical talent”, the assigning of which Giovanna Grana resented.
- The rest of the bestiary is likely referencing the rhetoric of “TERFs”, or “trans-exclusionary radical feminists”. Just as TERFs exclude transgender women with the claim to “protect women's spaces”, the Undead Sassy Witches exclude Giovanna with the claim to “protect witches' spaces”.

← OrochimarioUniconiglioNasnasty →
“
These long-lived cuties used to be so rare that only one captive example was recorded in a thousand years. The ancient world beheled it with awe and greeted it with "aww!" Fearing its extinction, our ancestors yearned to hear the creature's incredible mating call, produced by contracting a methane sac within its majestic horn.
That was then. This is now, a few years after the second Uniconiglio was caught. Now they outnumber us five to one. Now their methane wilts our crops. Now their horny song plays eternal and it sounds like cats on fire. Now extinction seems like a good idea and we no longer care if it's us or them.
— Ars Gouda, p. Bm 1
Uniconiglio is an enemy that appears in Red Moon Manor.

I can’t see them, but they see me. I can feel them. Watching. Always watching. The pressure of their gaze pushes down on me like earth on the grave. Who’s paranoid? Me? I’m not paranoid. You’re paranoid. Who’s looking at me from behind those lidless orbs?
— Ars Gouda, p. 104
Unknown is an enemy that appears in Moongolow, Cappella Magna, and Boss Rash. In Moongolow, it appears during the Lunar eclipse event, first as a boss and later also as a normal enemy. In Cappella Magna and Boss Rash, it only appears as a boss. When it appears as a boss it can drop a Treasure Chest.
Unknown's health is based on the player's level and it takes reduced knockback. It also does not collide with other enemies and is resistant to freeze, instant kill, and debuffs.

Venus is an enemy primarily encountered in Mad Forest, with an additional appearance in Boss Rash.
In Mad Forest, it first appears as a boss and later reappears as a normal enemy in later waves. In Boss Rash, it appears exclusively as a boss.
Venus is semi-resistant to knockback. Its boss variant has health that scales with the player's level and is partially resistant to freeze.
Defeating the Giant Blue Venus that spawns at 25:00 mark in Mad Forest for the first time unlocks the stage's Hyper Mode.

- Venus is one of the few enemies in the game to have an unlisted variant with an official name.
- Venus has the same statline as Mantichana and Big Mummy.

“
Said to have evolved from verminous creatures that flocked from a world trapped in time. Their lupine nature has been called into question, but if you ask for their opinion, they’ll tear you to shreds with a most uncommon brutality. They must be sensitive about it.

Werewolf is an enemy primarily encountered in Mad Forest, with additional appearances in Dairy Plant and Boss Rash.
In Mad Forest, it appears as both a normal enemy and a boss. In Boss Rash, it appears exclusively as a boss. In Dairy Plant, it spawns as a special enemy after collecting the Cheese pickup on the stage.
Werewolf takes reduced knockback. It has two distinct boss variants: Boss 1, also known as "Giant Werewolf", whose health scales with the player's level, and Boss 2, also known as "Colossal Werewolf", which does not.

← SkeletonZombieMudman →
“
“Why simply work the smallfolk to death? Work them beyond it!” This, the Cardinal’s necromancers laughed as they turned to the grave for a cheap alternative to labor that needed rest and food. Sadly for them, while their new workforce slept not, feeding was a whole other matter.
— Ars Gouda, p. 7
Zombie is an enemy primarily encountered in Mad Forest, with an additional appearance in Eudaimonia Machine.
In Mad Forest, it appears exclusively as a normal enemy. In Eudaimonia Machine, it is summoned by The Directer as a special enemy during the second phase of the stage.
Zombie takes reduced knockback.
